#!/usr/bin/env python3

"""Flood Ready Vehicle System — vehicle rental with flood-risk monitoring.

Everything is standard library: Tkinter for the interface, SQLite for storage.
No third-party packages, no network required, no web server. Data lives in a
folder inside your own user profile (or beside this file when run portably).

File layout
-----------
The file is organised as one top-down narrative so a reader can scroll from the
constants to the launcher without jumping around:

  1.  Constants & palette
  2.  Folders, logging, fonts, theme
  3.  Canvas primitives + UI building blocks (Button, Card, Stat, Table, ...)
  4.  Map data + GPS tracking feed + MapCanvas
  5.  Security helpers + formatting helpers
  6.  Schema, seed data, Store (the SQLite layer)
  7.  Record dialogs (Customer/Vehicle/Zone/Booking/Payment/User/Password)
  8.  Pages (Dashboard, Vehicles, Customers, Bookings, ... Settings)
  9.  Login window + Main window shell
  10. Smoke test + launcher
"""

def ui_scale() -> float:
    """Pixel-layout scale factor against the 96-DPI design target.

    Every font in this app is point-sized, so text grows with the display
    DPI (Windows 125-150% scaling renders it up to ~50% wider and taller
    than the design target) while geometry constants are plain pixels.
    Scaling the pixel side by the same ratio — read from the live body-font
    linespace, 16 px at the 96-DPI design — keeps the two in step on
    high-DPI displays. Clamped at 1.0 so standard displays stay
    pixel-for-pixel identical to the approved design.
    """
    body = FONTS.get("body")
    if body is None:
        return 1.0
    try:
        return max(1.0, body.metrics("linespace") / 16.0)
    except tk.TclError:  # no display yet (design-time preview)
        return 1.0


log = logging.getLogger("floodready")


def _safe_call(action: Callable[[], Any], *, what: str = "operation", silent: bool = True) -> None:
    """Run an action; log and optionally show any exception so the UI never crashes.

    Used for after-callbacks, button commands and feed ticks where a single
    failure must not take down the whole window. When ``silent=False`` (used
    for button clicks), the error is also shown to the user so a broken
    function is never silently ignored.
    """
    try:
        action()
    except tk.TclError:
        pass  # widget already destroyed — expected during shutdown
    except Exception as exc:
        log.exception("%s failed", what)
        if not silent:
            try:
                import traceback as _tb

                detail = "".join(_tb.format_exception(type(exc), exc, exc.__traceback__))[:500]
                messagebox.showerror(APP_NAME, f"{what}:\n\n{detail}", parent=None)
            except Exception:
                pass


def _safe_after(
    widget: tk.Misc, ms: int, callback: Callable[[], None], *, what: str = "timer"
) -> str:
    """Schedule a callback that cannot kill its timer chain on exception.

    Every recurring ``after`` loop in the app goes through this helper so a
    single bad tick (database locked, widget gone, …) does not silently stop
    the feed, the connectivity check or the auto scan.
    """

    def _run() -> None:
        if not widget.winfo_exists():
            return
        _safe_call(callback, what=what)

    return widget.after(ms, _run)


def debounce(widget: tk.Misc, ms: int = 200) -> Callable[[Callable[[], None]], None]:
    """Return a debounced scheduler that cancels the previous pending call.

    Replaces the three near-identical ``_schedule_refresh`` /
    ``_schedule_list_refresh`` methods with one shared helper.
    """
    state: dict[str, str | None] = {"job": None}

    def schedule(action: Callable[[], None]) -> None:
        previous = state.get("job")
        if previous is not None:
            try:
                widget.after_cancel(previous)
            except tk.TclError:
                pass

        def _run() -> None:
            state["job"] = None
            if widget.winfo_exists():
                _safe_call(action, what="debounced refresh")

        state["job"] = widget.after(ms, _run)

    return schedule


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Folders — %LOCALAPPDATA%\FloodReadyVehicleSystem with portable fallback
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def local_app_data_root() -> Path:
    """%LOCALAPPDATA%\\FloodReadyVehicleSystem (portable fallback beside the file)."""
    override = os.getenv("FLOODREADY_DATA_DIR")
    if override:
        return Path(override)
    local_app_data = os.getenv("LOCALAPPDATA")
    if local_app_data:
        return Path(local_app_data) / APP_SHORT_NAME
    home = os.getenv("XDG_DATA_HOME")
    if home:
        return Path(home) / APP_SHORT_NAME
    return Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "app_data" / APP_SHORT_NAME


DATA_ROOT = local_app_data_root()
DATA_DIR = DATA_ROOT / "data"
EXPORT_DIR = DATA_ROOT / "exports"
LOG_DIR = DATA_ROOT / "logs"
LOG_FILE = LOG_DIR / "application.log"
DATABASE_FILE = DATA_DIR / "rental_system.db"


def setup_logging(level: int = logging.INFO) -> Path:
    try:
        LOG_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    except OSError:  # pragma: no cover - defensive
        pass
    handler = RotatingFileHandler(LOG_FILE, maxBytes=1_000_000, backupCount=3, encoding="utf-8")
    handler.setFormatter(logging.Formatter("%(asctime)s | %(levelname)-8s | %(message)s"))
    root = logging.getLogger()
    root.setLevel(level)
    for existing in list(root.handlers):
        root.removeHandler(existing)
    root.addHandler(handler)
    log.info("%s %s starting | data=%s", APP_NAME, APP_VERSION, DATA_DIR)
    return LOG_FILE


def ensure_folders() -> None:
    for folder in (DATA_DIR, EXPORT_DIR, LOG_DIR):
        try:
            folder.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        except OSError:  # pragma: no cover - read-only profile
            fallback = Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "app_data" / folder.name
            fallback.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Fonts and theme — one ttk style, one font registry, applied to every window
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def build_fonts(root: tk.Tk) -> None:
    families = set(tkfont.families(root))
    default = str(tkfont.nametofont("TkDefaultFont").actual("family"))
    for name in (
        "Segoe UI",
        "SF Pro Text",
        "Helvetica Neue",
        "Inter",
        "Ubuntu",
        "DejaVu Sans",
        "Helvetica",
    ):
        if name in families:
            family = name
            break
    else:
        family = default
    for name in ("JetBrains Mono", "Cascadia Mono", "Consolas", "Menlo", "DejaVu Sans Mono"):
        if name in families:
            mono = name
            break
    else:
        mono = "Courier New"

    def font(size: int, weight: str = "normal", family_name: str = family) -> tkfont.Font:
        return tkfont.Font(root=root, family=family_name, size=size, weight=weight)

    FONTS.update(
        {
            "micro": font(8),
            "micro_bold": font(8, "bold"),
            "small": font(9),
            "small_bold": font(9, "bold"),
            "nav": font(10),
            "nav_bold": font(10, "bold"),
            "body": font(10),
            "h2": font(13, "bold"),
            "title": font(20, "bold"),
            "display": font(24, "bold"),
            "value": font(20, "bold"),
            "value_small": font(17, "bold"),
            "mono": font(9, family_name=mono),
            "mono_bold": font(9, "bold", family_name=mono),
        }
    )


def apply_theme(root: tk.Tk) -> ttk.Style:
    """Install the light, hairline theme used across every window."""
    style = ttk.Style(root)
    try:
        style.theme_use("clam")
    except tk.TclError:  # pragma: no cover
        pass
    p = PALETTE
    style.configure(
        ".", background=p["bg"], foreground=p["ink_soft"], font=FONTS["body"], borderwidth=0
    )

    for name, background in (
        ("Page.TFrame", p["bg"]),
        ("Card.TFrame", p["surface"]),
        ("Sunken.TFrame", p["sunken"]),
        ("CardBorder.TFrame", p["line"]),
        ("Bar.TFrame", p["surface"]),
        ("Sidebar.TFrame", p["sidebar"]),
        ("SidebarDeep.TFrame", p["sidebar_deep"]),
        ("SidebarBorder.TFrame", p["sidebar_line"]),
        ("SidebarCard.TFrame", p["sidebar_hover"]),
    ):
        style.configure(name, background=background)

    def labels(background: str, suffix: str) -> None:
        style.configure(
            f"Title{suffix}", background=background, foreground=p["ink"], font=FONTS["h2"]
        )
        style.configure(
            f"Heading{suffix}", background=background, foreground=p["ink"], font=FONTS["h2"]
        )
        style.configure(
            f"Body{suffix}", background=background, foreground=p["ink_soft"], font=FONTS["body"]
        )
        style.configure(
            f"Muted{suffix}", background=background, foreground=p["muted"], font=FONTS["small"]
        )
        style.configure(
            f"Micro{suffix}", background=background, foreground=p["faint"], font=FONTS["micro"]
        )
        style.configure(
            f"Value{suffix}", background=background, foreground=p["ink"], font=FONTS["value"]
        )

    labels(p["bg"], ".TLabel")
    labels(p["surface"], "Card.TLabel")
    labels(p["sunken"], "Sunken.TLabel")
    style.configure("Title.TLabel", background=p["bg"], foreground=p["ink"], font=FONTS["title"])

    style.configure(
        "BrandSidebar.TLabel",
        background=p["sidebar"],
        foreground=p["sidebar_ink"],
        font=FONTS["h2"],
    )
    style.configure(
        "MutedSidebar.TLabel",
        background=p["sidebar"],
        foreground=p["sidebar_muted"],
        font=FONTS["small"],
    )
    style.configure(
        "MicroSidebar.TLabel",
        background=p["sidebar"],
        foreground=p["sidebar_muted"],
        font=FONTS["micro"],
    )
    style.configure(
        "GroupSidebar.TLabel",
        background=p["sidebar"],
        foreground=p["sidebar_soft"],
        font=FONTS["micro_bold"],
    )
    style.configure(
        "BrandMarkSidebar.TLabel",
        background=p["sidebar_active"],
        foreground="#FFFFFF",
        font=FONTS["h2"],
    )
    style.configure(
        "TitleSidebarCard.TLabel",
        background=p["sidebar_hover"],
        foreground=p["sidebar_ink"],
        font=FONTS["small_bold"],
    )
    style.configure(
        "MutedSidebarCard.TLabel",
        background=p["sidebar_hover"],
        foreground=p["sidebar_muted"],
        font=FONTS["micro"],
    )

    def flat(
        name: str, background: str, foreground: str, hover: str, border: str | None = None
    ) -> None:
        edge = border or background
        style.configure(
            name,
            background=background,
            foreground=foreground,
            font=FONTS["small_bold"],
            bordercolor=edge,
            lightcolor=edge,
            darkcolor=edge,
            relief="flat",
            padding=(13, 8),
        )
        style.map(
            name,
            background=[("disabled", background), ("active", hover)],
            foreground=[("disabled", p["faint"])],
            bordercolor=[("active", hover)],
            lightcolor=[("active", hover)],
            darkcolor=[("active", hover)],
        )

    flat("Primary.TButton", p["accent"], "#FFFFFF", p["accent_hover"])
    flat("Quiet.TButton", p["accent_soft"], p["accent"], "#D6EAE0")
    flat("Ghost.TButton", p["sunken"], p["ink_soft"], "#E9EBE7")
    flat("Secondary.TButton", p["surface"], p["ink_soft"], p["sunken"], border=p["line"])
    flat("Danger.TButton", p["danger"], "#FFFFFF", "#A03A32")
    flat("Sidebar.TButton", p["sidebar"], p["sidebar_soft"], p["sidebar_hover"])
    flat("SidebarActive.TButton", p["sidebar_active"], "#FFFFFF", p["accent_hover"])
    flat(
        "SidebarGhost.TButton",
        p["sidebar_hover"],
        p["sidebar_soft"],
        p["sidebar_line"],
        border=p["sidebar_line"],
    )
    for sidebar_name in ("Sidebar.TButton", "SidebarActive.TButton"):
        style.configure(sidebar_name, anchor="w", padding=(16, 11))

    style.configure(
        "Field.TEntry",
        fieldbackground=p["surface"],
        background=p["surface"],
        foreground=p["ink"],
        bordercolor=p["line"],
        lightcolor=p["line"],
        darkcolor=p["line"],
        insertcolor=p["ink"],
        padding=(12, 10),
        relief="flat",
    )
    style.map(
        "Field.TEntry",
        bordercolor=[("focus", p["accent_line"])],
        lightcolor=[("focus", p["accent_line"])],
        darkcolor=[("focus", p["accent_line"])],
    )
    style.configure(
        "PasswordField.TEntry",
        fieldbackground=p["surface"],
        background=p["surface"],
        foreground=p["ink"],
        bordercolor=p["line"],
        lightcolor=p["line"],
        darkcolor=p["line"],
        insertcolor=p["ink"],
        # right padding reserves room for the in-field eye toggle and scales
        # with the font metrics so high-DPI text keeps its clearance
        padding=(12, 10, round(48 * max(1.0, FONTS["body"].metrics("linespace") / 13.0)), 10),
        relief="flat",
    )
    style.map(
        "PasswordField.TEntry",
        bordercolor=[("focus", p["accent_line"])],
        lightcolor=[("focus", p["accent_line"])],
        darkcolor=[("focus", p["accent_line"])],
    )
    style.configure(
        "Field.TCombobox",
        fieldbackground=p["surface"],
        background=p["surface"],
        foreground=p["ink"],
        bordercolor=p["line"],
        lightcolor=p["line"],
        darkcolor=p["line"],
        arrowsize=12,
        padding=(11, 8),
        relief="flat",
    )
    style.map(
        "Field.TCombobox",
        fieldbackground=[("readonly", p["surface"])],
        background=[("readonly", p["surface"])],
        bordercolor=[("focus", p["accent_line"])],
    )
    for option, value in (
        ("*TCombobox*Listbox.font", FONTS["small"]),
        ("*TCombobox*Listbox.background", p["surface"]),
        ("*TCombobox*Listbox.foreground", p["ink_soft"]),
        ("*TCombobox*Listbox.selectBackground", p["accent_soft"]),
        ("*TCombobox*Listbox.selectForeground", p["ink"]),
        ("*TCombobox*Listbox.borderWidth", 0),
        ("*TCombobox*Listbox.relief", "flat"),
        ("*TCombobox*Listbox.highlightThickness", 0),
    ):
        root.option_add(option, value)

    style.configure(
        "Data.Treeview",
        background=p["surface"],
        fieldbackground=p["surface"],
        foreground=p["ink_soft"],
        # row height follows the DPI-scaled font so cell text never crowds
        # the row borders on high-DPI displays
        rowheight=round(40 * ui_scale()),
        borderwidth=0,
        font=FONTS["small"],
    )
    style.configure(
        "Data.Treeview.Heading",
        background=p["sunken"],
        foreground=p["ink_soft"],
        font=FONTS["micro_bold"],
        relief="flat",
        borderwidth=0,
        # 8px side padding lines the header text up with the cell text below it
        padding=(8, round(12 * ui_scale())),
    )
    flat("ToggleOn.TButton", p["accent_soft"], p["accent"], "#D6EAE0", border=p["accent_line"])
    flat("ToggleOff.TButton", p["surface"], p["muted"], p["sunken"], border=p["line"])
    style.configure("ToggleOn.TButton", font=FONTS["micro_bold"], padding=(10, 5))
    style.configure("ToggleOff.TButton", font=FONTS["micro_bold"], padding=(10, 5))
    style.map(
        "Data.Treeview",
        background=[("selected", p["accent_soft"])],
        foreground=[("selected", p["ink"])],
    )
    style.map("Data.Treeview.Heading", background=[("active", p["surface"])])

    style.layout("Vertical.Thin.TScrollbar", style.layout("Vertical.TScrollbar"))
    style.configure(
        "Vertical.Thin.TScrollbar",
        background=p["line"],
        troughcolor=p["surface"],
        bordercolor=p["surface"],
        lightcolor=p["line"],
        darkcolor=p["line"],
        arrowsize=11,
        gripcount=0,
        relief="flat",
    )
    style.map(
        "Vertical.Thin.TScrollbar",
        background=[("active", p["faint"])],
        lightcolor=[("active", p["faint"])],
        darkcolor=[("active", p["faint"])],
    )

    style.configure(
        "Switch.TCheckbutton",
        background=p["surface"],
        foreground=p["ink_soft"],
        font=FONTS["small"],
        focuscolor=p["surface"],
    )
    style.map("Switch.TCheckbutton", background=[("active", p["surface"])])

    style.configure(
        "TitleBar.TLabel", background=p["surface"], foreground=p["ink"], font=FONTS["h2"]
    )
    style.configure(
        "MutedBar.TLabel", background=p["surface"], foreground=p["ink_soft"], font=FONTS["small"]
    )
    style.configure(
        "MicroBar.TLabel", background=p["surface"], foreground=p["muted"], font=FONTS["micro"]
    )
    style.configure(
        "AccentCard.TLabel", background=p["surface"], foreground=p["accent"], font=FONTS["small"]
    )
    style.configure(
        "DangerCard.TLabel", background=p["surface"], foreground=p["danger"], font=FONTS["small"]
    )
    style.configure(
        "FieldPlaceholder.TLabel",
        background=p["surface"],
        foreground=p["faint"],
        font=FONTS["small"],
    )

    style.configure(
        "Side.TButton",
        background=p["surface"],
        foreground=p["ink_soft"],
        font=FONTS["nav"],
        anchor="w",
        padding=(14, 7),
        relief="flat",
        bordercolor=p["surface"],
        lightcolor=p["surface"],
        darkcolor=p["surface"],
    )
    style.map(
        "Side.TButton",
        background=[("active", p["sunken"])],
        foreground=[("active", p["ink"])],
        lightcolor=[("active", p["sunken"])],
        darkcolor=[("active", p["sunken"])],
        bordercolor=[("active", p["sunken"])],
    )
    style.configure(
        "SideActive.TButton",
        background=p["accent_soft"],
        foreground=p["accent"],
        font=FONTS["small_bold"],
        anchor="w",
        padding=(14, 7),
        relief="flat",
        bordercolor=p["accent_soft"],
        lightcolor=p["accent_soft"],
        darkcolor=p["accent_soft"],
    )
    style.map(
        "SideActive.TButton",
        background=[("active", p["accent_soft"])],
        foreground=[("active", p["accent"])],
        lightcolor=[("active", p["accent_soft"])],
        darkcolor=[("active", p["accent_soft"])],
        bordercolor=[("active", p["accent_soft"])],
    )

    pills = {
        "PillLow": (RISK_COLORS["low"], "#E6EEE4"),
        "PillModerate": (RISK_COLORS["moderate"], "#F4EBDA"),
        "PillHigh": (RISK_COLORS["high"], "#F2E0D5"),
        "PillSevere": (RISK_COLORS["severe"], "#F2E2DE"),
        "PillInfo": (SEVERITY_COLORS["info"], p["info_soft"]),
        "PillWarning": (SEVERITY_COLORS["warning"], p["warn_soft"]),
        "PillCritical": (SEVERITY_COLORS["critical"], p["danger_soft"]),
        "PillNeutral": (p["muted"], p["sunken"]),
        "PillAccent": (p["accent"], p["accent_soft"]),
    }
    for name, (foreground, background) in pills.items():
        style.configure(
            f"{name}Card.TLabel",
            background=background,
            foreground=foreground,
            font=FONTS["micro_bold"],
            padding=(11, 5),
        )
    return style


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Canvas primitives + UI building blocks
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def round_rect(
    canvas: tk.Canvas, x1: float, y1: float, x2: float, y2: float, radius: float = 10, **kwargs: Any
) -> int:
    """Draw a rounded rectangle on a canvas (polygon with smoothed corners)."""
    radius = max(0.0, min(radius, (x2 - x1) / 2, (y2 - y1) / 2))
    points = [
        x1 + radius,
        y1,
        x2 - radius,
        y1,
        x2,
        y1,
        x2,
        y1 + radius,
        x2,
        y2 - radius,
        x2,
        y2,
        x2 - radius,
        y2,
        x1 + radius,
        y2,
        x1,
        y2,
        x1,
        y2 - radius,
        x1,
        y1 + radius,
        x1,
        y1,
    ]
    return canvas.create_polygon(points, smooth=True, **kwargs)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Rounded buttons — canvas-drawn so every control shares one crisp shape
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


# ttk style name -> visual recipe.  Callers keep using the familiar style
# names ("Primary.TButton", "Sidebar.TButton", ...) while the widget itself
# is a canvas that renders a true rounded rectangle with hover and press
# states — something ttk's flat rectangles cannot do.
BUTTON_KINDS: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {
    "Primary.TButton": dict(
        fill="accent",
        fg="#FFFFFF",
        hover="accent_hover",
        border=None,
        font="small_bold",
        height=38,
        padx=18,
        radius=10,
    ),
    "Secondary.TButton": dict(
        fill="surface",
        fg="ink_soft",
        hover="sunken",
        border="line",
        font="small_bold",
        height=38,
        padx=16,
        radius=10,
    ),
    "Quiet.TButton": dict(
        fill="accent_soft",
        fg="accent",
        hover="#D5E1D2",
        border=None,
        font="small_bold",
        height=38,
        padx=16,
        radius=10,
    ),
    "Ghost.TButton": dict(
        fill="sunken",
        fg="ink_soft",
        hover="line_soft",
        border=None,
        font="small_bold",
        height=34,
        padx=14,
        radius=9,
    ),
    "Danger.TButton": dict(
        fill="danger",
        fg="#FFFFFF",
        hover="#9A5F58",
        border=None,
        font="small_bold",
        height=38,
        padx=16,
        radius=10,
    ),
    "Sidebar.TButton": dict(
        fill="sidebar",
        fg="sidebar_soft",
        hover="sidebar_hover",
        border=None,
        font="nav",
        height=40,
        padx=14,
        radius=10,
        anchor="w",
    ),
    "SidebarActive.TButton": dict(
        fill="sidebar_active",
        fg="#FFFFFF",
        hover="accent_hover",
        border=None,
        font="nav_bold",
        height=40,
        padx=14,
        radius=10,
        anchor="w",
    ),
    "SidebarGhost.TButton": dict(
        fill="sidebar_hover",
        fg="sidebar_soft",
        hover="sidebar_line",
        border="sidebar_line",
        font="small",
        height=34,
        padx=12,
        radius=9,
    ),
    "ToggleOn.TButton": dict(
        fill="accent_soft",
        fg="accent",
        hover="#D5E1D2",
        border="accent_line",
        font="micro_bold",
        height=30,
        padx=13,
        radius=9,
    ),
    "ToggleOff.TButton": dict(
        fill="surface",
        fg="muted",
        hover="sunken",
        border="line",
        font="micro_bold",
        height=30,
        padx=13,
        radius=9,
    ),
    "Hero.TButton": dict(
        fill="accent",
        fg="#FFFFFF",
        hover="accent_hover",
        border=None,
        font="small_bold",
        height=48,
        padx=18,
        radius=12,
    ),
}

# ttk frames whose background colour the buttons must blend into.
_STYLE_BG: dict[str, str] = {
    "Page.TFrame": "bg",
    "Card.TFrame": "surface",
    "Sunken.TFrame": "sunken",
    "CardBorder.TFrame": "line",
    "Bar.TFrame": "surface",
    "Sidebar.TFrame": "sidebar",
    "SidebarDeep.TFrame": "sidebar_deep",
    "SidebarBorder.TFrame": "sidebar_line",
    "SidebarCard.TFrame": "sidebar_hover",
}


def _host_surface(widget: tk.Widget) -> str:
    """Best guess at the colour behind a button so its corners look cut out."""
    try:
        style_name = str(widget.cget("style"))
    except tk.TclError:
        style_name = ""
    if style_name in _STYLE_BG:
        return PALETTE[_STYLE_BG[style_name]]
    try:
        return str(widget.cget("background"))
    except (tk.TclError, KeyError):
        return PALETTE["surface"]


class Button(tk.Canvas):
    """Rounded flat button that speaks the ttk.Button dialect.

    Supports the keyword arguments used across this application — ``text``,
    ``command``, ``style`` and ``width`` (a minimum width in pixels) — and the
    ``configure(style=...)`` restyling the shell performs for the active page.
    The button is drawn as a smooth rounded rectangle with quiet hover and
    press states, so it reads as a modern control rather than a grey ttk slab.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        master: tk.Widget,
        text: str = "",
        command: Callable[[], None] | None = None,
        style: str = "Primary.TButton",
        width: int | None = None,
        **_ignored: Any,
    ) -> None:
        self._kind = style if style in BUTTON_KINDS else "Primary.TButton"
        self.text = str(text)
        self._command = command
        self._state = "normal"
        self._hover = False
        self._pressed = False
        self._min_width = int(width) if width else 0
        self._flash_job: str | None = None
        super().__init__(
            master, highlightthickness=0, bd=0, background=_host_surface(master), cursor="hand2"
        )
        self.bind("<Enter>", self._on_enter)
        self.bind("<Leave>", self._on_leave)
        self.bind("<Button-1>", self._on_press)
        self.bind("<Configure>", lambda _event: self._render())
        self._fit()
        self._render()

    # -- geometry ----------------------------------------------------------
    def _font(self) -> tkfont.Font:
        return FONTS[BUTTON_KINDS[self._kind]["font"]]

    def _fit(self) -> None:
        kind = BUTTON_KINDS[self._kind]
        try:
            text_width = self._font().measure(self.text)
        except tk.TclError:  # fonts not built yet (design-time preview)
            text_width = len(self.text) * 7
        width = max(int(text_width) + kind["padx"] * 2, self._min_width, 24)
        # height follows the DPI-scaled font so taller text never clips
        height = round(kind["height"] * ui_scale())
        if str(self.cget("width")) != str(width) or str(self.cget("height")) != str(height):
            super().configure(width=width, height=height)

    # -- painting ----------------------------------------------------------
    def _render(self) -> None:
        self.delete("all")
        kind = BUTTON_KINDS[self._kind]
        p = PALETTE
        disabled = self._state == "disabled"
        fill_key = kind["fill"] if kind["fill"] in p else None
        fill = p.get(fill_key, kind["fill"]) if fill_key else kind["fill"]
        if fill_key:
            hover = p[kind["hover"]] if kind["hover"] in p else kind["hover"]
        else:
            hover = kind["hover"]
        press = _mix(fill, "#000000", 0.16)
        if self._pressed and not disabled:
            fill = press
        elif self._hover and not disabled:
            fill = hover
        if kind.get("border"):
            edge = p[kind["border"]]
        else:
            edge = fill
        width = max(int(self.winfo_width()), 1)
        height = max(int(self.winfo_height()), 1) or kind["height"]
        round_rect(
            self, 0, 0, width - 1, height - 1, radius=kind["radius"], fill=fill, outline=edge
        )
        if disabled:
            # "muted" (not "faint") keeps disabled labels readable — the old
            # #B0A89B on the near-white surface was a ~2:1 contrast ratio
            colour = p["sidebar_muted"] if self._kind.startswith("Sidebar") else p["muted"]
        else:
            colour = p[kind["fg"]] if kind["fg"] in p else kind["fg"]
        anchor = kind.get("anchor", "center")
        x = kind["padx"] if anchor == "w" else width / 2
        self.create_text(
            x,
            height / 2 + 1,
            text=self.text,
            font=self._font(),
            fill=colour,
            anchor="w" if anchor == "w" else "center",
        )

    # -- interaction ---------------------------------------------------------
    def _on_enter(self, _event: tk.Event) -> None:
        self._hover = True
        self._render()

    def _on_leave(self, _event: tk.Event) -> None:
        self._hover = False
        self._pressed = False
        self._render()

    def _on_press(self, _event: tk.Event) -> None:
        if self._state == "disabled":
            return
        self._pressed = True
        self._render()
        if self._flash_job is not None:
            try:
                self.after_cancel(self._flash_job)
            except tk.TclError:
                pass
        try:
            self._flash_job = self.after(140, self._release_flash)
        except tk.TclError:
            self._flash_job = None
        if self._command is not None:
            _safe_call(self._command, what=f"button action: {self.text!r}", silent=False)

    def _release_flash(self) -> None:
        self._flash_job = None
        if self._pressed:
            self._pressed = False
            self._render()

    def invoke(self) -> None:
        if self._state == "normal" and self._command is not None:
            self._command()

    # -- ttk-compatible configuration ----------------------------------------
    def configure(self, cnf: Any = None, **kw: Any) -> None:
        if cnf is not None:
            kw = {**cnf, **kw}
        changed = False
        for key, value in kw.items():
            if key == "text":
                self.text = str(value)
                changed = True
            elif key == "command":
                self._command = value
                changed = True
            elif key == "state":
                self._state = str(value)
                super().configure(cursor="hand2" if self._state == "normal" else "arrow")
                changed = True
            elif key == "style":
                name = str(value)
                if name in BUTTON_KINDS and name != self._kind:
                    self._kind = name
                    changed = True
            elif key == "width":
                self._min_width = int(value)
                changed = True
            else:
                super().configure({key: value})
        if changed:
            self._fit()
            self._render()

    def cget(self, key: str) -> Any:
        if key == "text":
            return self.text
        if key == "state":
            return self._state
        if key == "style":
            return self._kind
        if key == "command":
            return self._command
        return super().cget(key)

    def keys(self) -> list[str]:  # pragma: no cover - introspection helper
        return ["text", "state", "style", "command", *super().keys()]


class Card(ttk.Frame):
    """Surface panel with a soft hairline border (ttk cannot draw one natively)."""

    def __init__(
        self, master: tk.Widget, padding: tuple[int, int] = (24, 22), style: str = "Card.TFrame"
    ) -> None:
        super().__init__(master, style="CardBorder.TFrame", padding=1)
        self.body = ttk.Frame(self, style=style, padding=padding)
        self.body.pack(fill="both", expand=True)


class Stat(ttk.Frame):
    """Metric tile: tinted icon chip, oversized value, quiet context line."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        master: tk.Widget,
        label: str,
        meta: str = "",
        tone: str = "accent",
        glyph: str = "◆",
        command: Callable[[], None] | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        super().__init__(master, style="CardBorder.TFrame", padding=1)
        body = ttk.Frame(self, style="Card.TFrame", padding=(24, 22))
        body.pack(fill="both", expand=True)
        top = ttk.Frame(body, style="Card.TFrame")
        top.pack(fill="x")
        chip_size = round(38 * ui_scale())
        chip = tk.Canvas(
            top,
            width=chip_size,
            height=chip_size,
            background=PALETTE["surface"],
            highlightthickness=0,
            bd=0,
        )
        chip.pack(side="left")
        round_rect(
            chip,
            1,
            1,
            chip_size - 1,
            chip_size - 1,
            radius=round(11 * ui_scale()),
            fill=PALETTE.get(f"{tone}_soft", PALETTE["accent_soft"]),
            outline="",
        )
        chip.create_text(
            chip_size / 2,
            chip_size / 2 + 1,
            text=glyph,
            font=FONTS["body"],
            fill=PALETTE.get(tone, PALETTE["accent"]),
        )
        ttk.Label(top, text=label.upper(), style="MicroCard.TLabel").pack(
            side="left", padx=(14, 0), pady=(15, 0)
        )
        self.value = ttk.Label(body, text="—", style="ValueCard.TLabel")
        self.value.pack(anchor="w", pady=(22, 0))
        self.meta = ttk.Label(
            body,
            text=meta,
            style="MutedCard.TLabel",
            wraplength=round(230 * ui_scale()),
            justify="left",
        )
        self.meta.pack(anchor="w", pady=(7, 0))
        if command is not None:
            for widget in (body, self.value, self.meta, top):
                widget.configure(cursor="hand2")
                widget.bind("<Button-1>", lambda _event: command())

    def set(self, value: str, meta: str = "") -> None:
        self.value.configure(text=value)
        if meta:
            self.meta.configure(text=meta)


class Bar(tk.Canvas):
    """Slim rounded progress meter that follows its container width."""

    def __init__(
        self, master: tk.Widget, height: int = 6, color: str | None = None, track: str | None = None
    ) -> None:
        super().__init__(
            master, height=height, background=track or PALETTE["sunken"], highlightthickness=0, bd=0
        )
        self.color = color or PALETTE["accent"]
        self.fraction = 0.0
        self.bind("<Configure>", lambda _event: self._draw())

    def set(self, fraction: float) -> None:
        self.fraction = max(0.0, min(1.0, fraction))
        self._draw()

    def _draw(self) -> None:
        self.delete("all")
        width = max(int(self.winfo_width()), 1)
        height = max(int(self.winfo_height()), 1)
        filled = int(width * self.fraction)
        if filled > 0:
            round_rect(
                self,
                0,
                0,
                max(filled, height),
                height,
                radius=height / 2,
                fill=self.color,
                outline="",
            )


class Bars(tk.Canvas):
    """Column chart drawn on a plain canvas — no chart library needed."""

    def __init__(self, master: tk.Widget, height: int = 150) -> None:
        super().__init__(
            master,
            height=round(height * ui_scale()),
            background=PALETTE["surface"],
            highlightthickness=0,
            bd=0,
        )
        self.data: list[tuple[str, float]] = []
        self.bind("<Configure>", lambda _event: self._draw())

    def set(self, data: list[tuple[str, float]]) -> None:
        self.data = data
        self._draw()

    def _draw(self) -> None:
        self.delete("all")
        width, height = max(int(self.winfo_width()), 40), max(int(self.winfo_height()), 40)
        if not self.data:
            return
        top, label_space = round(42 * ui_scale()), round(28 * ui_scale())
        baseline = height - label_space
        peak = max(value for _label, value in self.data)
        if peak <= 0:
            self.create_text(
                width / 2,
                height / 2,
                text="No transactions recorded yet",
                font=FONTS["small"],
                fill=PALETTE["faint"],
            )
            self.create_line(0, baseline, width, baseline, fill=PALETTE["line"], width=1)
            return
        count = len(self.data)
        gap = round(18 * ui_scale())
        column = max((width - gap * (count - 1)) / count, 8)
        # three faint horizontal guides (peak, 3/4, 1/2) give the eye reference
        for fraction in (1.0, 0.75, 0.5, 0.25):
            y = baseline - (baseline - top) * fraction
            self.create_line(0, y, width, y, fill=PALETTE["line_soft"], width=1, dash=(2, 4))
            # axis value label on the left edge — gives the chart a y-axis
            if fraction in (1.0, 0.5):
                label_value = peak * fraction
                self.create_text(
                    2,
                    y - 2,
                    text=_short(label_value),
                    anchor="nw",
                    font=FONTS["micro"],
                    fill=PALETTE["faint"],
                )
        self.create_line(0, baseline + 1, width, baseline + 1, fill=PALETTE["line"], width=1)
        peak_index = max(range(count), key=lambda index: self.data[index][1])
        for index, (label, value) in enumerate(self.data):
            x = index * (column + gap)
            bar_height = (baseline - top) * (value / peak)
            y = baseline - bar_height
            radius = max(0.0, min(column / 2, 5.0))
            color = PALETTE["accent"] if index == peak_index and value > 0 else "#A8BFA8"
            if bar_height > radius:
                round_rect(self, x, y, x + column, baseline, radius=radius, fill=color, outline="")
            elif bar_height > 0:
                self.create_rectangle(x, y, x + column, baseline, fill=color, outline="")
            if value > 0:
                self.create_text(
                    x + column / 2,
                    y - round(9 * ui_scale()),
                    text=_short(value),
                    font=FONTS["micro"],
                    fill=PALETTE["ink_soft"] if index == peak_index else PALETTE["muted"],
                    anchor="s",
                )
            self.create_text(
                x + column / 2,
                height - round(6 * ui_scale()),
                text=label,
                font=FONTS["small"],
                fill=PALETTE["ink_soft"] if index == peak_index else PALETTE["muted"],
                anchor="s",
            )


class Table(ttk.Frame):
    """Data table: heading row, striped rows, coloured status tags."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        master: tk.Widget,
        columns: Sequence[tuple[str, str, int, str]],
        on_select: Callable[[str], None] | None = None,
        on_double: Callable[[str], None] | None = None,
        height: int = 12,
    ) -> None:
        super().__init__(master, style="CardBorder.TFrame", padding=1)
        holder = ttk.Frame(self, style="Card.TFrame")
        holder.pack(fill="both", expand=True)
        self.keys = [column[0] for column in columns]
        self.tree = ttk.Treeview(
            holder,
            style="Data.Treeview",
            columns=self.keys,
            show="headings",
            selectmode="browse",
            height=height,
        )
        self._base_widths = [width for _, _, width, _ in columns]
        heading_font = FONTS.get("micro_bold") or tkfont.nametofont("TkHeadingFont")
        self._text_font = FONTS.get("small") or tkfont.nametofont("TkDefaultFont")
        # a column never shrinks below its own header text
        self._min_widths = [
            max(44, heading_font.measure(str(label).upper()) + 18) for _, label, _, _ in columns
        ]
        self._needs: list[int] = list(self._min_widths)
        for key, heading_text, width, anchor in columns:
            # headings align with their values — right-aligned numbers get
            # right-aligned headers instead of a ragged row of headers
            self.tree.heading(key, text=str(heading_text).upper(), anchor=anchor)
            self.tree.column(key, width=width, minwidth=44, anchor=anchor, stretch=True)
        # Adaptive columns: rescale to the tree's pixel width on every resize
        # so wide tables (bookings, vehicles) never push their last columns
        # past the right edge — every column stays readable.
        self.tree.bind("<Configure>", self._fit_columns, add=True)
        self.scroll = ttk.Scrollbar(
            holder, orient="vertical", command=self.tree.yview, style="Vertical.Thin.TScrollbar"
        )
        self.tree.configure(yscrollcommand=self.scroll.set)
        self.tree.pack(side="left", fill="both", expand=True, padx=(10, 0), pady=10)
        self.scroll.pack(side="right", fill="y", pady=10, padx=(0, 8))
        self.tree.tag_configure("odd", background=PALETTE["surface"])
        self.tree.tag_configure("even", background=PALETTE["line_soft"])
        for name, color in (
            ("ok", PALETTE["ok"]),
            ("warn", PALETTE["warn"]),
            ("danger", PALETTE["danger"]),
            ("info", PALETTE["info"]),
            ("accent", PALETTE["accent"]),
            ("muted", PALETTE["muted"]),
        ):
            self.tree.tag_configure(name, foreground=color)
        if on_select:
            self.tree.bind("<<TreeviewSelect>>", lambda _event: on_select(self.selected()))
        if on_double:
            self.tree.bind("<Double-1>", lambda _event: on_double(self.selected()))

    def _fit_columns(self, _event: tk.Event | None = None) -> None:
        """Give every column a fair share of the tree's actual pixel width.

        Columns first get what their content needs, spare room is shared out
        proportionally, and only when the card is genuinely too narrow do the
        widest columns give way — headers always stay readable.
        """
        available = self.tree.winfo_width() - 16  # keep a gutter before the scrollbar
        if available < 120:
            return
        needs = self._needs
        total = sum(needs)
        scale = available / total if total else 1.0
        if total <= available:
            widths = [max(44, round(need * scale)) for need in needs]
        else:
            widths = [
                max(minimum, round(need * scale)) for minimum, need in zip(self._min_widths, needs)
            ]
            # header minimums themselves overflow — shave the widest columns
            excess = sum(widths) - available
            while excess > 0:
                widest = max(range(len(widths)), key=lambda i: widths[i])
                slack = widths[widest] - self._min_widths[widest]
                if slack <= 0:
                    break
                step = min(excess, slack)
                widths[widest] -= step
                excess -= step
        for key, width in zip(self.keys, widths):
            self.tree.column(key, width=width, stretch=True)

    def clear(self) -> None:
        for item in self.tree.get_children(""):
            self.tree.delete(item)

    def set_rows(
        self, rows: Sequence[dict[str, Any]], tag_of: Callable[[dict[str, Any]], str] | None = None
    ) -> None:
        previous = self.selected()  # keep the highlighted row across refreshes
        self.clear()
        # measure what each column's content actually needs so _fit_columns can
        # distribute width by need, not by guesswork
        self._needs = [
            max(
                minimum,
                min(
                    320,
                    max((self._text_font.measure(str(row.get(key, ""))) for row in rows), default=0)
                    + 14,
                ),
            )
            for minimum, key in zip(self._min_widths, self.keys)
        ]
        for index, row in enumerate(rows):
            values = [row.get(key, "") for key in self.keys]
            tags = ["even" if index % 2 else "odd"]
            if tag_of:
                extra = tag_of(row)
                if extra:
                    tags.append(extra)
            self.tree.insert("", "end", iid=str(row.get("id", index)), values=values, tags=tags)
        if previous and self.tree.exists(previous):
            self.tree.selection_set(previous)
            self.tree.see(previous)
        self._fit_columns()

    def selected(self) -> str:
        selection = self.tree.selection()
        return selection[0] if selection else ""


class Field(ttk.Frame):
    """Label plus entry (or combobox) in one tidy block."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        master: tk.Widget,
        label: str,
        value: str = "",
        width: int = 26,
        choices: Sequence[str] | None = None,
        on_change: Callable[[str], None] | None = None,
        secret: bool = False,
        editable: bool = False,
    ) -> None:
        super().__init__(master, style="Card.TFrame")
        ttk.Label(self, text=label.upper(), style="MicroCard.TLabel").pack(anchor="w")
        self.variable = tk.StringVar(value=value)
        self.widget: tk.Widget
        if choices is None:
            self.widget = ttk.Entry(
                self,
                textvariable=self.variable,
                width=width,
                style="Field.TEntry",
                show="•" if secret else "",
            )
        else:
            self.widget = ttk.Combobox(
                self,
                textvariable=self.variable,
                values=list(choices),
                width=width,
                state="normal" if editable else "readonly",
                style="Field.TCombobox",
            )
        self.widget.pack(fill="x", pady=(7, 0))
        if on_change:
            self.variable.trace_add("write", lambda *_args: on_change(self.variable.get()))

    def get(self) -> str:
        return self.variable.get().strip()


class Divider(ttk.Frame):
    """One pixel hairline used inside cards."""

    def __init__(self, master: tk.Widget) -> None:
        super().__init__(master, style="CardBorder.TFrame", height=1)
        self.pack_propagate(False)


def empty_state_block(parent: tk.Widget, text: str, hint: str = "") -> None:
    """Centered glyph chip, message and optional hint for empty panels.

    Used wherever a card or detail column has nothing to show yet — the wrap
    length keeps the message inside narrow side panels instead of clipping.
    """
    spacer = ttk.Frame(parent, style="Card.TFrame")
    spacer.pack(fill="x", pady=(26, 0))
    glyph_size = round(44 * ui_scale())
    glyph = tk.Canvas(
        spacer,
        width=glyph_size,
        height=glyph_size,
        background=PALETTE["surface"],
        highlightthickness=0,
        bd=0,
    )
    glyph.pack()
    round_rect(
        glyph,
        2,
        2,
        glyph_size - 2,
        glyph_size - 2,
        radius=round(12 * ui_scale()),
        fill=PALETTE["accent_soft"],
        outline="",
    )
    glyph.create_text(
        glyph_size / 2, glyph_size / 2 + 1, text="◈", font=FONTS["h2"], fill=PALETTE["accent"]
    )
    # 176px wrap fits the narrowest detail panel (240px card minus 48px padding
    # plus a margin) so the message breaks instead of clipping at the edge
    ttk.Label(
        parent,
        text=text,
        style="TitleCard.TLabel",
        wraplength=round(176 * ui_scale()),
        justify="center",
        anchor="center",
    ).pack(fill="x", pady=(14, 0))
    if hint:
        ttk.Label(
            parent,
            text=hint,
            style="MutedCard.TLabel",
            wraplength=round(176 * ui_scale()),
            justify="center",
            anchor="center",
        ).pack(fill="x", pady=(4, 0))


def heading(
    parent: tk.Widget, title: str, subtitle: str, actions: Sequence[tk.Widget] = ()
) -> ttk.Frame:
    """Page header: large title, quiet subtitle, actions on the right, hairline below."""
    bar = ttk.Frame(parent, style="Page.TFrame")
    text = ttk.Frame(bar, style="Page.TFrame")
    text.pack(side="left", fill="x", expand=True)
    ttk.Label(text, text=title, style="Title.TLabel").pack(anchor="w")
    subtitle_label = ttk.Label(text, text=subtitle, style="Muted.TLabel", justify="left")
    subtitle_label.pack(anchor="w", pady=(9, 0))
    if actions:
        tools = ttk.Frame(bar, style="Page.TFrame")
        tools.place(in_=bar, relx=1.0, rely=0.5, anchor="e", x=-2)
        for widget in actions:
            widget.pack(in_=tools, side="left", padx=(10, 0))
            widget.tk.call("raise", widget._w)

        # The subtitle must wrap before it can run underneath the action
        # buttons: the wrap budget tracks the header width minus the button
        # cluster, so long subtitles break onto a second line instead of
        # colliding with the buttons.
        scale = ui_scale()

        def _fit_subtitle(_event: tk.Event | None = None) -> None:
            budget = max(
                round(280 * scale), bar.winfo_width() - tools.winfo_reqwidth() - round(36 * scale)
            )
            if subtitle_label.cget("wraplength") != budget:
                subtitle_label.configure(wraplength=budget)

        bar.bind("<Configure>", _fit_subtitle, add=True)
        bar.after_idle(_fit_subtitle)
    bar.pack(fill="x", pady=(2, 12))
    ttk.Frame(parent, style="CardBorder.TFrame", height=1).pack(fill="x", pady=(0, 18))
    return bar


def grid_row(
    parent: tk.Widget,
    makers: Sequence[Callable[[tk.Widget], tk.Widget]],
    weights: Sequence[int] | None = None,
    gap: int = 14,
) -> tuple[ttk.Frame, list[tk.Widget]]:
    """Build a row of panels; weights decide how extra width is shared."""
    wrap = ttk.Frame(parent, style="Page.TFrame")
    wrap.pack(fill="both", expand=True, pady=(0, gap))
    weights = list(weights) if weights else [1] * len(makers)
    items: list[tk.Widget] = []
    for index, make in enumerate(makers):
        item = make(wrap)
        # minwidth ensures no column gets squeezed below its proportional share
        # — without this, a Treeview's natural width can push a sibling column
        # to near-zero even with a high weight.
        wrap.columnconfigure(index, weight=weights[index], minsize=240)
        item.grid(row=0, column=index, sticky="nsew", padx=(0 if index == 0 else gap, 0))
        items.append(item)
    wrap.rowconfigure(0, weight=1)
    return wrap, items


def card_title(card: Card, title: str, hint: str = "", action: tk.Widget | None = None) -> None:
    head = ttk.Frame(card.body, style="Card.TFrame")
    head.pack(fill="x")
    left = ttk.Frame(head, style="Card.TFrame")
    left.pack(side="left", fill="x", expand=True)
    ttk.Label(left, text=title, style="TitleCard.TLabel").pack(anchor="w")
    if hint:
        ttk.Label(left, text=hint, style="MutedCard.TLabel").pack(anchor="w", pady=(4, 0))
    if action is not None:
        action.pack(side="right")

KM_PER_DEG_LAT = 110.574
CENTER_LAT = 14.6
KM_PER_DEG_LON = 111.320 * math.cos(math.radians(CENTER_LAT))


def _ll(x: float, y: float) -> tuple[float, float]:
    """Convert a point on the design grid to (latitude, longitude)."""
    return 14.68 - y / 5132.0, 120.92 + x / 4997.0


def _path(points: Sequence[tuple[float, float]]) -> list[tuple[float, float]]:
    return [_ll(x, y) for x, y in points]

COMPASS = (
    "N",
    "NNE",
    "NE",
    "ENE",
    "E",
    "ESE",
    "SE",
    "SSE",
    "S",
    "SSW",
    "SW",
    "WSW",
    "W",
    "WNW",
    "NW",
    "NNW",
)


_HASH_CACHE: dict[tuple[int, ...], float] = {}


def _hash(*keys: int) -> float:
    """Stable pseudo-random value in [0, 1) so the map never flickers.

    Cached by argument tuple — the map's block fabric and GPS backfill call
    _hash with the same (i, j, k) tuples on every redraw.
    """
    key_tuple = keys
    cached = _HASH_CACHE.get(key_tuple)
    if cached is not None:
        return cached
    value = 17
    for key in keys:
        value = (value * 1_000_003 + int(key) * 2_654_435_761) % 4_294_967_296
    result = (value % 100_000) / 100_000
    _HASH_CACHE[key_tuple] = result
    return result


_MIX_CACHE: dict[tuple[str, str, int], str] = {}
_MIX_QUANT = 16  # quantize amount to 1/16 steps so cache hits are common


def _mix(first: str, second: str, amount: float) -> str:
    """Blend two #RRGGBB colours; 0 returns the first, 1 the second.

    Results are cached at 1/16 quantization — the draw loop calls _mix hundreds
    of times per frame for the same handful of (base, tint, alpha) tuples, so
    caching turns each into a dict lookup.
    """
    amount = max(0.0, min(1.0, amount))
    key = (first, second, round(amount * _MIX_QUANT))
    cached = _MIX_CACHE.get(key)
    if cached is not None:
        return cached
    a = round(amount * _MIX_QUANT) / _MIX_QUANT

    def channel(index: int) -> int:
        start = int(first[index : index + 2], 16)
        end = int(second[index : index + 2], 16)
        return round(start + (end - start) * a)

    result = f"#{channel(1):02X}{channel(3):02X}{channel(5):02X}"
    _MIX_CACHE[key] = result
    return result


def compass_text(bearing: float) -> str:
    return COMPASS[int(((bearing % 360) + 11.25) // 22.5) % 16]


def _nearest_vertex(lat: float, lon: float) -> tuple[int, int, bool]:
    """Road index, vertex index and travel direction for the closest road point."""
    best: tuple[float, int, int, bool] | None = None
    for road_index, (_name, points) in enumerate(ARTERIALS):
        for vertex, (point_lat, point_lon) in enumerate(points):
            kilometres = distance_km(lat, lon, point_lat, point_lon)
            if best is None or kilometres < best[0]:
                best = (kilometres, road_index, vertex, vertex <= len(points) // 2)
    assert best is not None
    return best[1], best[2], best[3]


def _nearest_end(lat: float, lon: float, used: set[int]) -> tuple[int, int, bool] | None:
    best: tuple[float, int, int, bool] | None = None
    for road_index, (_name, points) in enumerate(ARTERIALS):
        if road_index in used:
            continue
        for vertex, forward in ((0, True), (len(points) - 1, False)):
            point_lat, point_lon = points[vertex]
            kilometres = distance_km(lat, lon, point_lat, point_lon)
            if best is None or kilometres < best[0]:
                best = (kilometres, road_index, vertex, forward)
    if best is None:
        return None
    return best[1], best[2], best[3]


def _route_for(lat: float, lon: float, hops: int = 4) -> list[tuple[float, float]]:
    """Chain a few roads together so a unit drives a believable route."""
    road_index, vertex, forward = _nearest_vertex(lat, lon)
    path: list[tuple[float, float]] = []
    used: set[int] = set()
    for _step in range(hops):
        if road_index is None or road_index >= len(ARTERIALS):
            break
        used.add(road_index)
        _name, points = ARTERIALS[road_index]
        leg = list(points[vertex:]) if forward else list(reversed(points[: vertex + 1]))
        if path:
            leg = leg[1:]
        path.extend(leg)
        if not path:
            break
        found = _nearest_end(path[-1][0], path[-1][1], used)
        if found is None:
            break
        road_index, vertex, forward = found
    cleaned: list[tuple[float, float]] = []
    for point in path:
        if not cleaned or point != cleaned[-1]:
            cleaned.append(point)
    if len(cleaned) < 2:
        cleaned = [(lat, lon), (lat + 0.005, lon + 0.005)]
    return cleaned


class Unit:
    """One vehicle following a route along the road network."""

    def __init__(self, record: dict[str, Any], path: list[tuple[float, float]]) -> None:
        self.id = int(record["id"])
        self.category = str(record["category"])
        self.plate = str(record["plate_number"])
        # Units in the shop do not move; available units are only repositioned.
        self.status = str(record["status"])
        throttle = {"rented": 1.0, "available": 0.32, "maintenance": 0.0}.get(self.status, 0.5)
        base = UNIT_SPEEDS.get(self.category, 26.0) * throttle
        self.speed = base * (0.85 + 0.4 * _hash(self.id, 13))
        self.parked = self.speed <= 0.01
        # Start exactly on the saved GPS pin: prepend it to the route so the
        # first frame agrees with the fleet list and the stored coordinates,
        # instead of snapping to the nearest road vertex.
        origin = (float(record["latitude"]), float(record["longitude"]))
        if tuple(path[0]) != origin:
            path = [origin, *path]
        self.path = path
        self.distances: list[float] = []
        total = 0.0
        for index in range(len(path) - 1):
            total += distance_km(
                path[index][0], path[index][1], path[index + 1][0], path[index + 1][1]
            )
            self.distances.append(total)
        self.total = max(total, 0.001)
        self.offset = 0.0
        self.direction = 1.0
        self.lat, self.lon = origin
        self.heading = 0.0
        self.trail: list[tuple[float, float]] = []
        self.distance_travelled = 0.0
        self._place()

    def advance(self, seconds: float, multiplier: float) -> None:
        if self.parked:
            return
        kilometres = self.speed * seconds * multiplier / 3600.0
        self.offset += kilometres * self.direction
        if self.offset >= self.total:
            self.offset = self.total
            self.direction = -1.0
        elif self.offset <= 0.0:
            self.offset = 0.0
            self.direction = 1.0
        self.distance_travelled += kilometres
        self._place()
        self.trail.append((self.lat, self.lon))
        if len(self.trail) > 40:
            self.trail.pop(0)

    def _place(self) -> None:
        previous = 0.0
        index = 0
        for position, reached in enumerate(self.distances):
            if reached >= self.offset:
                index = position
                break
            previous = reached
            index = position
        start = self.path[index]
        end = self.path[index + 1] if index + 1 < len(self.path) else start
        span = self.distances[index] - previous if index < len(self.distances) else 0.0
        fraction = 0.0 if span <= 0 else max(0.0, min(1.0, (self.offset - previous) / span))
        self.lat = start[0] + (end[0] - start[0]) * fraction
        self.lon = start[1] + (end[1] - start[1]) * fraction
        north = (end[0] - start[0]) * KM_PER_DEG_LAT
        east = (end[1] - start[1]) * KM_PER_DEG_LON
        if abs(north) + abs(east) > 1e-9:
            self.heading = math.degrees(math.atan2(east, north)) % 360.0


class TrackingFeed:
    """Simulated GPS feed: every unit drives a route at a realistic speed."""

    TICK_MS = 90
    COMMIT_SECONDS = 15.0
    DRAW_MIN_SECONDS = 0.11  # feed-driven repaints cap near 9 fps; physics stays at 90 ms

    def __init__(self, store: "Store", host: tk.Misc, speed: float = 4.0) -> None:
        self.store = store
        self.host = host
        self.speed = speed
        self.running = False
        self.units: dict[int, Unit] = {}
        self._known: dict[int, tuple[Any, ...]] = {}
        self.subscribers: list["MapCanvas"] = []
        self._job: str | None = None
        self._last: datetime | None = None
        self._since_commit = 0.0
        self._last_draw: datetime | None = None
        self.elapsed = 0.0
        self.rebuild()

    # -- setup ------------------------------------------------------------
    def rebuild(self) -> None:
        self.units = {}
        self._known = {}
        for record in self.store.vehicles():
            self._admit(record)

    def _admit(self, record: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
        vehicle_id = int(record["id"])
        if record["latitude"] is None or record["longitude"] is None:
            self.units.pop(vehicle_id, None)
            self._known.pop(vehicle_id, None)
            return
        fingerprint = (
            str(record["plate_number"]),
            str(record["status"]),
            str(record["category"]),
            round(float(record["latitude"]), 4),
            round(float(record["longitude"]), 4),
        )
        if self._known.get(vehicle_id) == fingerprint and vehicle_id in self.units:
            return
        route = _route_for(float(record["latitude"]), float(record["longitude"]))
        self.units[vehicle_id] = Unit(record, route)
        self._known[vehicle_id] = fingerprint

    def sync(self) -> None:
        current_ids: set[int] = set()
        for record in self.store.vehicles():
            vehicle_id = int(record["id"])
            current_ids.add(vehicle_id)
            self._admit(record)
        for vehicle_id in set(self.units) - current_ids:
            self.units.pop(vehicle_id, None)
            self._known.pop(vehicle_id, None)

    def subscribe(self, canvas: "MapCanvas") -> None:
        if canvas not in self.subscribers:
            self.subscribers.append(canvas)

    def unsubscribe(self, canvas: "MapCanvas") -> None:
        if canvas in self.subscribers:
            self.subscribers.remove(canvas)

    # -- control ----------------------------------------------------------
    def start(self) -> None:
        if self.running:
            return
        self.running = True
        self._last = None
        self._schedule()

    def stop(self) -> None:
        self.running = False
        if self._job is not None:
            try:
                self.host.after_cancel(self._job)
            except tk.TclError:  # pragma: no cover
                pass
            self._job = None

    def toggle(self) -> bool:
        if self.running:
            self.stop()
        else:
            self.start()
        return self.running

    def set_speed(self, value: float) -> None:
        self.speed = max(0.25, min(120.0, value))

    # -- loop -------------------------------------------------------------
    def _schedule(self) -> None:
        if not self.running:
            return
        try:
            self._job = _safe_after(self.host, self.TICK_MS, self._tick, what="tracking feed tick")
        except tk.TclError:
            self.running = False

    def _tick(self) -> None:
        if not self.running:
            return
        try:
            now = datetime.now()
            seconds = 0.0
            if self._last is not None:
                seconds = min((now - self._last).total_seconds(), 1.5)
            self._last = now
            moved = False
            if seconds > 0:
                self.elapsed += seconds
                for unit in self.units.values():
                    unit.advance(seconds, self.speed)
                moved = any(not unit.parked for unit in self.units.values())
                self._since_commit += seconds
                if self._since_commit >= self.COMMIT_SECONDS:
                    self.commit()
            if moved and (
                self._last_draw is None
                or (now - self._last_draw).total_seconds() >= self.DRAW_MIN_SECONDS
            ):
                self._last_draw = now
                for canvas in list(self.subscribers):
                    if not canvas.winfo_exists():
                        self.unsubscribe(canvas)
                        continue
                    try:
                        if canvas.winfo_ismapped():
                            canvas.draw()
                    except tk.TclError:
                        self.unsubscribe(canvas)
                    except Exception:
                        log.exception("feed canvas draw failed")
                        self.unsubscribe(canvas)
        except Exception:
            log.exception("tracking feed tick failed")
        finally:
            self._schedule()

    def commit(self) -> None:
        self._since_commit = 0.0
        if not self.units:
            return
        stamp = datetime.now().strftime(DATETIME_FORMAT)
        payload = [(unit.lat, unit.lon, stamp, unit.id) for unit in self.units.values()]
        try:
            # One connection and one transaction for the whole fleet — the old
            # per-unit commits opened a hundred connections every fifteen seconds.
            with self.store.connect() as connection:
                connection.executemany(
                    "UPDATE vehicles SET latitude = ?, longitude = ?, updated_at = ? WHERE id = ?",
                    payload,
                )
                connection.commit()
        except RuntimeError as error:
            # A transient database problem must never kill the feed loop; the
            # next commit interval will simply try again.
            log.warning("Live position commit skipped: %s", error)
            return
        for unit in self.units.values():
            known = self._known.get(unit.id)
            if known is not None:
                # Keep the fingerprint in step with the persisted position so
                # sync() does not mistake normal driving for a manual pin edit.
                self._known[unit.id] = known[:3] + (round(unit.lat, 4), round(unit.lon, 4))

    # -- reporting --------------------------------------------------------
    def status(self) -> str:
        # kept deliberately short so it never collides with the speed pills on
        # narrow windows — the active speed is already shown by the pills
        if not self.running:
            return f"Paused · {len(self.units)} units"
        speeds = [unit.speed for unit in self.units.values()] or [0.0]
        mean = sum(speeds) / len(speeds)
        return f"{len(self.units)} units · {mean:.0f} km/h"

    def live(self, vehicle_id: int) -> Unit | None:
        return self.units.get(vehicle_id)


class MapCanvas(tk.Canvas):
    """Vector basemap of Metro Manila with flood zones and fleet markers.

    The snapshot is read from the database once per ``reload()`` and cached, so
    dragging and zooming never touch SQLite. The projection is equirectangular
    with a per-axis kilometre scale, which keeps flood-zone rings round and
    true to scale at any zoom level.
    """

    MIN_SPAN = 1.2
    MAX_SPAN = 90.0
    HOME = (120.9890, 14.6050, 13.0)

    def __init__(
        self,
        master: tk.Widget,
        store: "Store",
        height: int = 320,
        on_select: Callable[[int], None] | None = None,
        on_click: Callable[[tuple[float, float]], None] | None = None,
        show_labels: bool = True,
        show_zones: bool = True,
        show_vehicles: bool = True,
        show_grid: bool = True,
        show_bookings: bool = False,
        show_customers: bool = False,
        legend: bool = True,
        border: bool = True,
        feed: "TrackingFeed | None" = None,
        detail: str = "medium",
    ) -> None:
        super().__init__(
            master,
            height=height,
            background=PALETTE["water"],
            highlightthickness=1 if border else 0,
            highlightbackground=PALETTE["line"],
            bd=0,
            takefocus=True,
        )
        self.store = store
        self.on_select = on_select
        self.on_click = on_click
        self.show_labels = show_labels
        self.show_zones = show_zones
        self.show_vehicles = show_vehicles
        self.show_grid = show_grid
        self.show_bookings = show_bookings
        self.show_customers = show_customers
        self.legend = legend
        self.detail = detail
        self.feed = feed
        self.center_lon, self.center_lat, self.span_km = self.HOME
        self.selected_id: int | None = None
        self.hover_id: int | None = None
        self.readout: tuple[float, float] = (self.center_lat, self.center_lon)
        self._drag: tuple[float, float] | None = None
        self._moved = False
        self._markers: dict[int, tuple[float, float]] = {}
        self._vehicles: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
        self._zones: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
        self._bookings: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
        self._customers: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
        self._anim: dict[str, Any] | None = None
        self._anim_job: str | None = None
        self._readout_box: int | None = None
        self._readout_text_id: int | None = None
        # layered rendering: the basemap only repaints when the view or the
        # data snapshot changes; feed ticks redraw just the live layer
        self._snapshot_version = 0
        self._base_signature: tuple[Any, ...] | None = None
        self._readout_shown: str | None = None

        self.bind("<Button-1>", self._on_press)
        self.bind("<B1-Motion>", self._on_drag)
        self.bind("<ButtonRelease-1>", self._on_release)
        self.bind("<Motion>", self._on_motion)
        self.bind("<Leave>", lambda _event: self._set_readout(None))
        self.bind("<Double-1>", self._on_double)
        self.bind("<MouseWheel>", self._on_wheel)
        self.bind("<Button-4>", lambda _event: self.zoom_by(0.86))
        self.bind("<Button-5>", lambda _event: self.zoom_by(1.16))
        self.bind("<Configure>", lambda _event: self.draw())
        for key in ("<Left>", "<Right>", "<Up>", "<Down>"):
            self.bind(key, self._on_arrow)
        for key in ("<plus>", "<equal>", "<KP_Add>"):
            self.bind(key, lambda _event: self.zoom_by(0.72))
        for key in ("<minus>", "<KP_Subtract>"):
            self.bind(key, lambda _event: self.zoom_by(1.38))
        self.bind("<Key-0>", lambda _event: self.reset())
        self.bind("<Key-f>", lambda _event: self.fit_fleet())
        self.bind("<Escape>", lambda _event: self.select(None))
        self.bind("<Destroy>", self._on_destroy)
        self.reload()
        if feed is not None:
            feed.subscribe(self)

    def _on_destroy(self, _event: tk.Event) -> None:
        self._cancel_anim()
        if self.feed is not None:
            self.feed.unsubscribe(self)

    # -- projection -------------------------------------------------------
    @property
    def scale(self) -> float:
        """Pixels per kilometre — identical on both axes."""
        return max(self.winfo_width(), 10) / self.span_km

    def project(self, lat: float, lon: float) -> tuple[float, float]:
        """(lat, lon) → canvas pixels, relative to the current camera centre.

        Both axes must measure from ``center_lat``/``center_lon`` — the y axis
        used to be pinned to the fixed CENTER_LAT constant, which silently
        broke vertical panning, follow-cam and zoom-to-point.
        """
        width, height = self.winfo_width(), self.winfo_height()
        x_km = (lon - self.center_lon) * KM_PER_DEG_LON
        y_km = (self.center_lat - lat) * KM_PER_DEG_LAT
        return width / 2 + x_km * self.scale, height / 2 + y_km * self.scale

    def unproject(self, x: float, y: float) -> tuple[float, float]:
        width, height = self.winfo_width(), self.winfo_height()
        x_km = (x - width / 2) / self.scale
        y_km = (y - height / 2) / self.scale
        return self.center_lat - y_km / KM_PER_DEG_LAT, self.center_lon + x_km / KM_PER_DEG_LON

    def visible(self, lat: float, lon: float, margin: float = 0.0) -> bool:
        x, y = self.project(lat, lon)
        return not self._outside(x, y, margin)

    # -- data -------------------------------------------------------------
    def reload(self) -> None:
        """Read a fresh snapshot from the database, then repaint."""
        self._vehicles = self.store.vehicles()
        self._zones = self.store.zones()
        self._bookings = (
            [
                row
                for row in self.store.bookings()
                if row.get("latitude") is not None and row.get("longitude") is not None
            ]
            if self.show_bookings
            else []
        )
        self._customers = (
            [
                row
                for row in self.store.customers()
                if row.get("latitude") is not None and row.get("longitude") is not None
            ]
            if self.show_customers
            else []
        )
        self._snapshot_version += 1
        self.draw()

    # -- interaction ------------------------------------------------------
    def _on_press(self, event: tk.Event) -> None:
        self._cancel_anim()
        self._drag = (event.x, event.y)
        self._moved = False
        self.focus_set()
        self._apply_cursor()

    def _on_drag(self, event: tk.Event) -> None:
        if not self._drag:
            return
        dx, dy = self._drag[0] - event.x, self._drag[1] - event.y
        if abs(dx) + abs(dy) > 2:
            self._moved = True
            self.follow_id = None  # a manual pan takes the camera off follow
        self.center_lon += dx / self.scale / KM_PER_DEG_LON
        self.center_lat += dy / self.scale / KM_PER_DEG_LAT
        self._drag = (event.x, event.y)
        self._apply_cursor()
        self.draw()

    def _on_release(self, event: tk.Event) -> None:
        self._drag = None
        self._apply_cursor()
        if self._moved:
            return
        if self.on_click is not None:
            self.on_click(self.unproject(event.x, event.y))
            return
        self.select(self.hover_id, notify=True)

    def _on_motion(self, event: tk.Event) -> None:
        self._set_readout(self.unproject(event.x, event.y))
        found = self._marker_at(event.x, event.y)
        if found != self.hover_id:
            self.hover_id = found
            self._apply_cursor()
            self.draw()

    def _marker_at(self, x: float, y: float) -> int | None:
        """Nearest vehicle marker within an eleven-pixel radius of the cursor."""
        best: tuple[float, int] | None = None
        for vehicle_id, (marker_x, marker_y) in self._markers.items():
            distance = (x - marker_x) ** 2 + (y - marker_y) ** 2
            if distance <= 121.0 and (best is None or distance < best[0]):
                best = (distance, vehicle_id)
        if best is not None:
            return best[1]
        if self.feed is None:
            return None
        # At high feed speeds the cached marker positions trail the live ones;
        # measure against the live feed before giving up.
        for vehicle in self._vehicles:
            if vehicle.get("latitude") is None or vehicle.get("longitude") is None:
                continue
            unit = self.feed.live(int(vehicle["id"]))
            if unit is None:
                continue
            live_x, live_y = self.project(unit.lat, unit.lon)
            if self._outside(live_x, live_y, 12):
                continue
            distance = (x - live_x) ** 2 + (y - live_y) ** 2
            if distance <= 121.0 and (best is None or distance < best[0]):
                best = (distance, int(vehicle["id"]))
        return best[1] if best is not None else None

    def _on_wheel(self, event: tk.Event) -> None:
        self.zoom_by(0.86 if event.delta > 0 else 1.16, at=(event.x, event.y))

    def _on_double(self, event: tk.Event) -> None:
        lat, lon = self.unproject(event.x, event.y)
        self.fly_to(lon, lat, self.span_km * 0.55)

    def _on_arrow(self, event: tk.Event) -> None:
        step = 70 / self.scale
        moves = {
            "Left": (-step, 0.0),
            "Right": (step, 0.0),
            "Up": (0.0, step),
            "Down": (0.0, -step),
        }
        dx, dy = moves.get(event.keysym, (0.0, 0.0))
        self.center_lon += dx / KM_PER_DEG_LON
        self.center_lat += dy / KM_PER_DEG_LAT
        self.draw()

    def _apply_cursor(self) -> None:
        if self._drag is not None:
            cursor = "fleur"
        elif self.hover_id is not None:
            cursor = "hand2"
        else:
            cursor = "crosshair"
        self.configure(cursor=cursor)

    # -- zoom and animation ----------------------------------------------
    def zoom_by(self, factor: float, at: tuple[float, float] | None = None) -> None:
        """Change the span, keeping the point under the cursor still."""
        self._cancel_anim()
        new_span = max(self.MIN_SPAN, min(self.MAX_SPAN, self.span_km * factor))
        if at is not None and new_span != self.span_km:
            width, height = self.winfo_width(), self.winfo_height()
            off_x_km = (at[0] - width / 2) / self.scale
            off_y_km = (at[1] - height / 2) / self.scale
            ratio = new_span / self.span_km
            self.center_lon += off_x_km * (1 - ratio) / KM_PER_DEG_LON
            self.center_lat -= off_y_km * (1 - ratio) / KM_PER_DEG_LAT
        self.span_km = new_span
        self.draw()

    def fly_to(self, lon: float, lat: float, span_km: float | None = None, ms: int = 340) -> None:
        """Ease the view towards a new centre and span."""
        self._cancel_anim()
        start = (self.center_lon, self.center_lat, self.span_km)
        end = (lon, lat, span_km if span_km else self.span_km)
        if sum(abs(start[index] - end[index]) for index in range(3)) < 1e-6:
            self.draw()
            return
        self._anim = {
            "start": start,
            "end": end,
            "steps": max(5, min(28, int(ms / 16))),
            "index": 0,
        }
        self._step_anim()

    def _step_anim(self) -> None:
        state = self._anim
        if state is None:
            return
        state["index"] += 1
        progress = min(state["index"] / state["steps"], 1.0)
        eased = 1 - (1 - progress) ** 3
        (s_lon, s_lat, s_span), (e_lon, e_lat, e_span) = state["start"], state["end"]
        self.center_lon = s_lon + (e_lon - s_lon) * eased
        self.center_lat = s_lat + (e_lat - s_lat) * eased
        self.span_km = s_span * (e_span / s_span) ** eased
        self.draw()
        if progress >= 1.0:
            self._anim = None
            self._anim_job = None
            return
        self._anim_job = self.after(16, self._step_anim)

    def _cancel_anim(self) -> None:
        self._anim = None
        if self._anim_job is not None:
            try:
