class PasswordEntry(ttk.Frame):
    """Entry with an in-field eye toggle for showing or hiding the password.

        The eye glyph sits inside the field's right padding (48 px at the 96-DPI
    design target, scaling with font metrics, reserved by the
        ``PasswordField.TEntry`` style) so it reads as a native affordance rather
        than a separate button row. ttk does not expose the entry interior, so the
        toggle is a small canvas placed over the field's right edge with ``place``.
    """

    def __init__(self, master: tk.Widget, textvariable: tk.StringVar, **kwargs: Any) -> None:
        super().__init__(master, style="Card.TFrame")
        self._shown = False
        kwargs.setdefault("style", "PasswordField.TEntry")
        kwargs.setdefault("show", "•")
        self.entry = ttk.Entry(self, textvariable=textvariable, **kwargs)
        self.entry.pack(fill="x", expand=True)
        eye_size = round(24 * ui_scale())
        self.eye = tk.Canvas(
            self,
            width=eye_size,
            height=eye_size,
            background=PALETTE["surface"],
            highlightthickness=0,
            bd=0,
            cursor="hand2",
        )
        self.eye.place(relx=1.0, rely=0.5, anchor="e", x=-round(8 * ui_scale()))
        self.eye.bind("<Button-1>", lambda _event: self.toggle())
        self.eye.bind("<Enter>", lambda _event: self._draw_eye(hover=True))
        self.eye.bind("<Leave>", lambda _event: self._draw_eye(hover=False))
        self._draw_eye(hover=False)

    def toggle(self) -> None:
        self._shown = not self._shown
        self.entry.configure(show="" if self._shown else "•")
        self._draw_eye()

    def focus_set(self) -> None:
        self.entry.focus_set()

    def _draw_eye(self, hover: bool = False) -> None:
        self.eye.delete("all")
        if self._shown:
            base = PALETTE["accent"]
            pupil = PALETTE["accent_hover"]
        else:
            # ink_soft for better contrast against the warm surface
            base = PALETTE["ink_soft"]
            pupil = PALETTE["ink_soft"] if hover else PALETTE["ink_soft"]
        size = max(2, int(self.eye.cget("width")))
        cx, cy = size / 2, size / 2
        unit = size / 24.0  # the glyph was drawn for a 24 px canvas
        # almond eye outline
        self.eye.create_oval(
            cx - 7 * unit,
            cy - 4 * unit,
            cx + 7 * unit,
            cy + 4 * unit,
            outline=base,
            width=1.6 * unit,
        )
        # pupil
        self.eye.create_oval(
            cx - 2.4 * unit,
            cy - 2.4 * unit,
            cx + 2.4 * unit,
            cy + 2.4 * unit,
            fill=pupil,
            outline="",
        )
        if not self._shown:
            # subtle slash — this eye is closed
            self.eye.create_line(
                cx - 7 * unit,
                cy - 4 * unit,
                cx + 7 * unit,
                cy + 4 * unit,
                fill=PALETTE["faint"],
                width=1.5 * unit,
            )


class LoginWindow(tk.Toplevel):
    """Split sign-in window — dark slate brand panel on the left, warm form on the right.

    The window itself sits inside a soft outer frame so the dark brand panel
    reads as an inset card rather than a raw window edge. Both panels are laid
    out from MEASURED font metrics rather than fixed pixel rows, so Windows
    DPI scaling (which renders fonts up to ~50% wider/taller than the 96-DPI
    design target) can never clip or overlap the text.
    """

    WINDOW_W = 960
    WINDOW_H = 580
    PANEL_W = 440
    OUTER_PAD = 16
    FEATURES = (
        ("⌖", "Live GPS tracking", "Every unit streams its position along the road network."),
        ("◈", "Flood-zone exposure", "Instant alerts the moment a unit drifts near a risk area."),
        ("▦", "Offline-first records", "SQLite storage with CSV export — no internet required."),
    )

    def __init__(self, master: tk.Tk, store: Store) -> None:
        super().__init__(master)
        self.store = store
        self.user: dict[str, Any] | None = None
        self.title(f"{APP_NAME} — Sign in")
        # outer background — gives the window a soft frame instead of a hard
        # edge butting directly against the dark brand panel
        self.configure(background=PALETTE["bg"])
        self.resizable(False, False)

        # soft outer frame in the page background tone, then a 1px hairline
        # border around the actual content card
        outer = ttk.Frame(self, style="Page.TFrame", padding=self.OUTER_PAD)
        outer.pack(fill="both", expand=True)
        shell = ttk.Frame(outer, style="CardBorder.TFrame", padding=1)
        shell.pack(fill="both", expand=True)
        content = ttk.Frame(shell, style="Card.TFrame")
        content.pack(fill="both", expand=True)

        self._build_brand_panel(content)
        self._build_form(content)

        self.bind("<Return>", lambda _event: self.sign_in())
        self.protocol("WM_DELETE_WINDOW", self.close)
        self.geometry(f"{self.WINDOW_W}x{self.WINDOW_H}")
        self._centre()

    # -- left panel ---------------------------------------------------------
    def _build_brand_panel(self, parent: tk.Widget) -> None:
        # Laid out by MEASUREMENT, not fixed offsets: every text block is drawn
        # anchored top-left, its rendered bounding box is read back from the
        # canvas, and the next block is stacked below that. Windows DPI scaling
        # renders fonts taller/wider than the 96-DPI design target, wrapping
        # lines the design assumed would not wrap — fixed pixel rows overlap
        # badly there, measured rows cannot.
        panel = tk.Canvas(
            parent,
            width=self.PANEL_W,
            height=self.WINDOW_H,
            background=PALETTE["sidebar_deep"],
            highlightthickness=0,
            bd=0,
        )
        panel.pack(side="left", fill="y")
        panel.pack_propagate(False)
        p = PALETTE
        # gaps scale with the live font metrics so a high-DPI display keeps
        # the same breathing room as the 96-DPI design (13 px ≈ body linespace
        # at 96 DPI; clamped at 1.0 so normal displays are unchanged).
        scale = max(1.0, FONTS["body"].metrics("linespace") / 13.0)
        # left/right content margins
        margin_x = 44
        text_right = self.PANEL_W - margin_x  # right edge of wrapped text
        body_wrap = text_right - margin_x  # wrap width for subtitle / footer
        # soft concentric rings anchored off the upper-right corner — a quiet
        # corner motif that stays clear of the text column at any font size.
        anchor_x = self.PANEL_W + 20
        anchor_y = -24
        ring_color = _mix(p["sidebar_line"], p["sidebar_deep"], 0.55)
        for radius in (70, 120, 170):
            panel.create_oval(
                anchor_x - radius,
                anchor_y - radius,
                anchor_x + radius,
                anchor_y + radius,
                outline=ring_color,
                width=1,
            )
        # accent halo — the innermost visible arc gets a slightly warmer tone
        panel.create_oval(
            anchor_x - 44,
            anchor_y - 44,
            anchor_x + 44,
            anchor_y + 44,
            outline=_mix(p["sidebar_active"], p["sidebar_deep"], 0.5),
            width=1.2,
        )
        # brand chip — small, at the very top of the panel
        chip_y = 48
        round_rect(
            panel,
            margin_x,
            chip_y,
            margin_x + 44,
            chip_y + 44,
            radius=13,
            fill=p["sidebar_active"],
            outline="",
        )
        panel.create_text(margin_x + 22, chip_y + 23, text="◈", font=FONTS["h2"], fill="#FFFFFF")

        def draw_text(
            y: float, text: str, font: tkfont.Font, fill: str, wrap: int, x: float | None = None
        ) -> float:
            """Draw one wrapped text block anchored top-left; return its bottom edge."""
            item = panel.create_text(
                margin_x if x is None else x,
                y,
                text=text,
                font=font,
                fill=fill,
                anchor="nw",
                width=wrap,
                justify="left",
            )
            box = panel.bbox(item)
            return box[3] if box else y + 16

        # brand title — the point size is auto-fit: shrunk step by step until
        # the widest line fits the panel, so no Windows font/DPI combination
        # can ever clip it.
        title_font = FONTS["title"].copy()
        while (
            title_font.measure("Vehicle System") > body_wrap and int(title_font.cget("size")) > 13
        ):
            title_font.configure(size=int(title_font.cget("size")) - 1)

        y = chip_y + 44 + round(32 * scale)
        y = draw_text(y, "Flood Ready", title_font, p["sidebar_ink"], body_wrap) + round(4 * scale)
        y = draw_text(y, "Vehicle System", title_font, p["sidebar_ink"], body_wrap) + round(
            20 * scale
        )
        y = draw_text(
            y,
            "Rental operations with flood-risk monitoring for Metro Manila.",
            FONTS["small"],
            p["sidebar_muted"],
            body_wrap,
        ) + round(36 * scale)

        # feature stack — each row is placed below the measured bottom of the
        # previous one, with a hairline divider between items.
        chip_size = 34
        text_x = margin_x + chip_size + 14
        feature_wrap = text_right - text_x  # wrap width for feature descriptions
        for index, (glyph, title, description) in enumerate(self.FEATURES):
            if index:
                panel.create_line(margin_x, y, text_right, y, fill=p["sidebar_line"], width=1)
                y += round(20 * scale)
            # icon chip, top-aligned with the feature title
            round_rect(
                panel,
                margin_x,
                y,
                margin_x + chip_size,
                y + chip_size,
                radius=9,
                fill=p["sidebar_hover"],
                outline="",
            )
            panel.create_text(
                margin_x + chip_size / 2,
                y + chip_size / 2 + 1,
                text=glyph,
                font=FONTS["body"],
                fill="#A8C5B0",
            )
            bottom = draw_text(
                y + 1, title, FONTS["small_bold"], p["sidebar_ink"], feature_wrap, x=text_x
            )
            bottom = max(
                bottom,
                draw_text(
                    bottom + round(3 * scale),
                    description,
                    FONTS["micro"],
                    p["sidebar_muted"],
                    feature_wrap,
                    x=text_x,
                ),
            )
            y = max(bottom, y + chip_size) + round(24 * scale)

        # grow the canvas when high-DPI metrics push the stack past the design
        # height (the window follows via its required-size logic in _centre),
        # then pin the version footer above the bottom edge.
        canvas_h = max(self.WINDOW_H, y + round(48 * scale))
        panel.configure(height=canvas_h)
        panel.create_text(
            margin_x,
            canvas_h - round(40 * scale),
            text=f"v{APP_VERSION}  ·  Tkinter + SQLite  ·  Offline-first",
            font=FONTS["micro"],
            fill=p["sidebar_muted"],
            anchor="w",
            width=body_wrap,
            justify="left",
        )

    # -- right panel ----------------------------------------------------------
    def _build_form(self, parent: tk.Widget) -> None:
        # paddings scale with the live font metrics for the same reason as the
        # brand panel — high-DPI Windows keeps the designed breathing room.
        scale = max(1.0, FONTS["body"].metrics("linespace") / 13.0)
        panel = ttk.Frame(parent, style="Card.TFrame", padding=(56, 16))
        panel.pack(side="left", fill="both", expand=True)
        # shrink-wrapped column, top-centred
        column = ttk.Frame(panel, style="Card.TFrame")
        column.pack(anchor="n", pady=(round(68 * scale), 0))

        ttk.Label(column, text="WELCOME BACK", style="AccentCard.TLabel").pack(anchor="w")
        tk.Label(
            column,
            text="Sign in",
            font=FONTS["title"],
            fg=PALETTE["ink"],
            background=PALETTE["surface"],
        ).pack(anchor="w", pady=(round(8 * scale), 0))
        ttk.Label(
            column,
            text="Use your staff account to continue.",
            style="MutedCard.TLabel",
            wraplength=380,
            justify="left",
        ).pack(anchor="w", pady=(round(8 * scale), 0))

        self.username = tk.StringVar(master=self, value=DEFAULT_ADMIN_USERNAME)
        self.password = tk.StringVar(master=self, value=DEFAULT_ADMIN_PASSWORD)
        self.error = tk.StringVar(master=self, value="")

        # consistent vertical rhythm (all values scale with the font metrics):
        # 32 px from the subtitle to the first field label, 24 px between the
        # two field groups, 8 px from each label to its entry, 20 px from the
        # password field to the sign-in button, tighter demo-account footer.
        ttk.Label(column, text="USERNAME", style="MicroCard.TLabel").pack(
            anchor="w", pady=(round(32 * scale), 0)
        )
        username_entry = ttk.Entry(column, textvariable=self.username, style="Field.TEntry")
        username_entry.pack(fill="x", pady=(round(8 * scale), 0))

        ttk.Label(column, text="PASSWORD", style="MicroCard.TLabel").pack(
            anchor="w", pady=(round(24 * scale), 0)
        )
        self.password_entry = PasswordEntry(column, self.password)
        self.password_entry.pack(fill="x", pady=(round(8 * scale), 0))

        # typing again after a failed attempt clears the stale message
        self.username.trace_add("write", lambda *_args: self.error.set(""))
        self.password.trace_add("write", lambda *_args: self.error.set(""))

        # the error line only exists while there is something to say — a
        # permanent empty label would leave a dead gap above the sign-in button
        self.error_label = ttk.Label(
            column,
            textvariable=self.error,
            style="DangerCard.TLabel",
            wraplength=380,
            justify="left",
        )
        self.error.trace_add("write", lambda *_args: self._sync_error())
        Button(column, text="Sign in", style="Hero.TButton", command=self.sign_in).pack(
            fill="x", pady=(round(20 * scale), 0)
        )

        ttk.Frame(column, style="CardBorder.TFrame", height=1).pack(
            fill="x", pady=(round(28 * scale), 0)
        )
        ttk.Label(
            column,
            text=f"Demo account · {DEFAULT_ADMIN_USERNAME} / {DEFAULT_ADMIN_PASSWORD}",
            style="MicroCard.TLabel",
            justify="left",
            wraplength=340,
        ).pack(anchor="w", pady=(round(16 * scale), 0))
        # truncate DATA_ROOT by measured pixel width (not character count) so
        # the line always fits one row at any DPI; the target stays narrower
        # than the input fields so this footer never dictates the column width
        # and always keeps its right margin
        target = max(240, username_entry.winfo_reqwidth() - 16)
        root_text = str(DATA_ROOT)
        if FONTS["micro"].measure(f"Local data: {root_text}") > target:
            while root_text and FONTS["micro"].measure(f"Local data: ...{root_text}") > target:
                root_text = root_text[1:]
            root_text = f"…{root_text}"
        ttk.Label(
            column, text=f"Local data: {root_text}", style="MicroCard.TLabel", justify="left"
        ).pack(anchor="w", pady=(round(4 * scale), 0))

        username_entry.focus_set()

    def _sync_error(self) -> None:
        """Show the error line between the password field and the button."""
        if self.error.get():
            self.error_label.pack(
                anchor="w",
                pady=(round(14 * max(1.0, FONTS["body"].metrics("linespace") / 13.0)), 0),
                after=self.password_entry,
            )
        else:
            self.error_label.pack_forget()

    def sign_in(self) -> None:
        try:
            self.user = self.store.login(self.username.get(), self.password.get())
        except ValueError as error:
            # clearing the password also clears the message via the variable
            # trace, so set the message last or it would never be seen
            self.password.set("")
            self.error.set(str(error))
            return
        except RuntimeError as error:
            self.error.set(str(error))
            return
        log.info("Signed in as %s", self.user["username"])
        self.destroy()

    def close(self) -> None:
        self.user = None
        self.destroy()

    def _centre(self) -> None:
        self.update_idletasks()
        width, height = max(self.WINDOW_W, self.winfo_reqwidth()), max(
            self.WINDOW_H, self.winfo_reqheight()
        )
        x = max(0, (self.winfo_screenwidth() - width) // 2)
        y = max(0, (self.winfo_screenheight() - height) // 3)
        self.geometry(f"{width}x{height}+{x}+{y}")


class MainWindow(ttk.Frame):
    """Sidebar shell with a menu bar, status bar and one page at a time."""

    def __init__(self, master: tk.Tk, store: Store, user: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
        super().__init__(master, style="Page.TFrame")
        self.store = store
        self.user = user
        self.pages: dict[str, Page] = {}
        self.current: str = ""
        self.buttons: dict[str, Button] = {}
        self.sign_out = False
        self._closing = threading.Event()
        self._online: bool | None = None

        master.title(f"{APP_NAME} — {user['full_name']}")
        # Window and minimum size scale with the display DPI so the point-sized
        # fonts keep their designed breathing room on high-DPI displays
        # (Windows 125-150% scaling). The window opens at the 1280x800 design
        # size or ~92% of the display — whichever is larger — so large monitors
        # get the extra room, and short screens are never overflowed (the old
        # clamp let an 800 px window exceed a 768 px laptop panel).
        scale = ui_scale()
        screen_w, screen_h = master.winfo_screenwidth(), master.winfo_screenheight()
        width = min(max(round(1280 * scale), round(screen_w * 0.92)), screen_w - 24)
        height = min(max(round(800 * scale), round(screen_h * 0.9)), screen_h - 64)
        master.geometry(
            f"{width}x{height}+{max(0, (screen_w - width) // 2)}+{max(0, (screen_h - height) // 2)}"
        )
        master.minsize(min(round(1080 * scale), width), min(round(680 * scale), height))
        master.protocol("WM_DELETE_WINDOW", self.close)
        self.feed = TrackingFeed(store, master)
        self._build_menu(master)
        self._build_status_bar()
        self._build_sidebar()
        self._bind_shortcuts(master)

        self.container = ttk.Frame(self, style="Page.TFrame")
        self.container.pack(side="left", fill="both", expand=True)
        self.show_page("dashboard")
        self.pack(fill="both", expand=True)
        # Give the window one paint cycle before the feed kicks in.
        _safe_after(self, 350, self._start_feed, what="feed start")
        self._start_connectivity_check()
        self._start_auto_scan()

    def _start_feed(self) -> None:
        try:
            self.feed.start()
        except tk.TclError:
            pass

    @staticmethod
    def _pack_separator(bar: ttk.Widget, side: str = "left") -> None:
        """Pack a 1px hairline separator into the status bar."""
        sep = tk.Canvas(
            bar, width=1, height=15, background=PALETTE["surface"], highlightthickness=0, bd=0
        )
        sep.create_line(0, 0, 0, 15, fill=PALETTE["line"], width=1)
        sep.pack(side=side, padx=18)

    # -- keyboard shortcuts -----------------------------------------------
    def _bind_shortcuts(self, master: tk.Tk) -> None:
        master.bind("<F5>", lambda _e: _safe_call(self.refresh_all, what="F5 refresh"))
        master.bind("<Control-n>", lambda _e: _safe_call(self.open_new_booking, what="Ctrl+N"))
        master.bind("<Control-N>", lambda _e: _safe_call(self.open_new_booking, what="Ctrl+N"))
        master.bind("<Control-p>", lambda _e: _safe_call(self.open_payment_dialog, what="Ctrl+P"))
        master.bind("<Control-P>", lambda _e: _safe_call(self.open_payment_dialog, what="Ctrl+P"))
        master.bind(
            "<Control-KeyPress-k>", lambda _e: _safe_call(self.open_command_palette, what="Ctrl+K")
        )
        master.bind(
            "<Control-Shift-K>", lambda _e: _safe_call(self.open_command_palette, what="Ctrl+K")
        )
        page_keys = {
            "1": "dashboard",
            "2": "vehicles",
            "3": "customers",
            "4": "bookings",
            "5": "transactions",
            "6": "maintenance",
            "7": "gps",
            "8": "zones",
            "9": "alerts",
        }
        for key, page in page_keys.items():
            master.bind(
                f"<Alt-{key}>",
                lambda _e, p=page: _safe_call(lambda: self.show_page(p), what=f"Alt+{key}"),
            )

    def open_command_palette(self) -> None:
        palette = tk.Toplevel(self)
        palette.title("Command Palette")
        palette.configure(background=PALETTE["bg"])
        palette.transient(self.winfo_toplevel())
        palette.resizable(False, False)
        frame = ttk.Frame(palette, style="CardBorder.TFrame", padding=1)
        frame.pack(fill="both", expand=True, padx=12, pady=12)
        body = ttk.Frame(frame, style="Card.TFrame", padding=(16, 14))
        body.pack(fill="both", expand=True)
        ttk.Label(body, text="COMMAND PALETTE", style="MicroCard.TLabel").pack(anchor="w")
        tk.Label(
            body, text="Jump to page", font=FONTS["h2"], fg=PALETTE["ink"], bg=PALETTE["surface"]
        ).pack(anchor="w", pady=(4, 0))
        search_var = tk.StringVar()
        entry = ttk.Entry(body, textvariable=search_var, style="Field.TEntry")
        entry.pack(fill="x", pady=(12, 0))
        entry.focus_set()
        # Results scroll in a canvas so every command stays reachable even
        # when the palette is clamped shorter than the full list (high-DPI
        # display, small screen) — the wheel router drives it like every
        # other scroller in the app.
        results_holder = ttk.Frame(body, style="Card.TFrame")
        results_holder.pack(fill="both", expand=True, pady=(10, 0))
        results_canvas = tk.Canvas(
            results_holder, background=PALETTE["surface"], highlightthickness=0, bd=0
        )
        results_canvas.pack(side="left", fill="both", expand=True)
        results_frame = ttk.Frame(results_canvas, style="Card.TFrame")
        results_window = results_canvas.create_window((0, 0), window=results_frame, anchor="nw")
        results_frame.bind(
            "<Configure>",
            lambda _event: results_canvas.configure(scrollregion=results_canvas.bbox("all")),
        )
        results_canvas.bind(
            "<Configure>",
            lambda event: results_canvas.itemconfigure(results_window, width=event.width),
        )
        results_scroller = WheelScroller(results_canvas, results_frame)
        palette.bind("<Destroy>", lambda _event: results_scroller.destroy(), add="+")
        commands = [(label, key) for key, label, _icon, _page in NAV_PAGES]
        commands.append(("New booking", "__new_booking"))
        commands.append(("Record payment", "__payment"))
        commands.append(("Backup database", "__backup"))
        buttons: list = []

        def rebuild(pattern: str = "") -> None:
            for child in results_frame.winfo_children():
                child.destroy()
            buttons.clear()
            pl = pattern.lower()
            for label, key in commands:
                if pl and pl not in label.lower():
                    continue
                btn = Button(
                    results_frame,
                    text=label,
                    style="Ghost.TButton",
                    command=lambda k=key: execute(k),
                )
                btn.pack(fill="x", pady=2)
                buttons.append(btn)

        def execute(key: str) -> None:
            palette.destroy()
            if key == "__new_booking":
                self.open_new_booking()
            elif key == "__payment":
                self.open_payment_dialog()
            elif key == "__backup":
                self._backup()
            else:
                self.show_page(key)

        search_var.trace_add("write", lambda *_: rebuild(search_var.get()))
        rebuild()
        # Requisitions settle at idle time: settle once, sync the results
        # canvas to the list's real height (a canvas with a window item
        # defaults to ~1 px on its own), then let the toplevel re-measure so
        # the requested size below reports the true content.
        palette.update_idletasks()
        results_canvas.configure(height=results_frame.winfo_reqheight())
        palette.update_idletasks()
        # Size to the DPI-scaled content now that the results exist: never
        # smaller than the 480x420 design, never taller than the main window,
        # and snapped to whole result rows so the fold never cuts a button in
        # half (the fixed 420 px height used to hide ~8 of the 15 commands).
        scale = ui_scale()
        host = self.winfo_toplevel()
        width = max(round(480 * scale), palette.winfo_reqwidth())
        wanted = max(round(420 * scale), palette.winfo_reqheight())
        budget = max(host.winfo_height() - round(80 * scale), round(420 * scale))
        if wanted > budget and buttons:
            # header = everything except the list; fit as many whole rows
            # as the budget allows instead of slicing through a button
            header_h = max(0, palette.winfo_reqheight() - results_frame.winfo_reqheight())
            row_step = max(1, buttons[0].winfo_reqheight() + 4)
            height = min(wanted, header_h + max(1, (budget - header_h) // row_step) * row_step)
        else:
            height = wanted
        palette.geometry(f"{width}x{height}")
        x = host.winfo_x() + (host.winfo_width() - width) // 2
        y = host.winfo_y() + round(120 * scale)
        y = max(0, min(y, palette.winfo_screenheight() - height - 12))
        palette.geometry(f"+{max(0, x)}+{y}")
        palette.bind("<Escape>", lambda _e: palette.destroy())
        palette.bind("<Return>", lambda _e: buttons and buttons[0].invoke())
        palette.grab_set()

    # -- chrome -----------------------------------------------------------
    def _build_menu(self, master: tk.Tk) -> None:
        menubar = tk.Menu(
            master,
            activebackground=PALETTE["accent_soft"],
            activeforeground=PALETTE["ink"],
            activeborderwidth=0,
            background=PALETTE["surface"],
            foreground=PALETTE["ink_soft"],
            font=FONTS["nav"],
            bd=0,
            relief="flat",
        )
        file_menu = tk.Menu(
            menubar,
            tearoff=0,
            activebackground=PALETTE["accent_soft"],
            activeforeground=PALETTE["ink"],
            activeborderwidth=0,
            background=PALETTE["surface"],
            foreground=PALETTE["ink_soft"],
            font=FONTS["nav"],
            bd=0,
            relief="flat",
        )
        file_menu.add_command(label="Backup database...", command=self._backup)
        file_menu.add_separator()
        file_menu.add_command(label="Sign out", command=self._sign_out)
        file_menu.add_command(label="Exit", command=self.close)
        menubar.add_cascade(label="File", menu=file_menu)
        help_menu = tk.Menu(
            menubar,
            tearoff=0,
            activebackground=PALETTE["accent_soft"],
            activeforeground=PALETTE["ink"],
            activeborderwidth=0,
            background=PALETTE["surface"],
            foreground=PALETTE["ink_soft"],
            font=FONTS["nav"],
            bd=0,
            relief="flat",
        )
        help_menu.add_command(label=f"About {APP_NAME}", command=self._about)
        menubar.add_cascade(label="Help", menu=help_menu)
        master.configure(menu=menubar)

    def _build_sidebar(self) -> None:
        sidebar = ttk.Frame(self, style="Sidebar.TFrame", width=round(262 * ui_scale()))
        sidebar.pack(side="left", fill="y")
        sidebar.pack_propagate(False)

        brand = ttk.Frame(sidebar, style="Sidebar.TFrame", padding=(24, 26))
        brand.pack(fill="x")
        head = ttk.Frame(brand, style="Sidebar.TFrame")
        head.pack(fill="x")
        mark_size = round(44 * ui_scale())
        mark = tk.Canvas(
            head,
            width=mark_size,
            height=mark_size,
            background=PALETTE["sidebar"],
            highlightthickness=0,
            bd=0,
        )
        mark.pack(side="left")
        round_rect(
            mark,
            1,
            1,
            mark_size - 1,
            mark_size - 1,
            radius=round(13 * ui_scale()),
            fill=PALETTE["sidebar_active"],
            outline="",
        )
        mark.create_text(
            mark_size / 2, mark_size / 2 + 1, text="◈", font=FONTS["h2"], fill="#FFFFFF"
        )
        name = ttk.Frame(head, style="Sidebar.TFrame")
        name.pack(side="left", padx=(14, 0))
        ttk.Label(name, text="Flood Ready", style="BrandSidebar.TLabel").pack(anchor="w")
        ttk.Label(name, text="Vehicle System", style="BrandSidebar.TLabel").pack(anchor="w")
        ttk.Label(
            brand, text=f"FLEET OPERATIONS · v{APP_VERSION}", style="MicroSidebar.TLabel"
        ).pack(anchor="w", pady=(13, 0))

        # The nav sits in a scrollable canvas: on high-DPI displays paired
        # with a small screen the scaled menu can be taller than the window,
        # and the app-wide wheel router already knows how to drive anything
        # registered alongside the page scrollers.
        nav_canvas = tk.Canvas(sidebar, background=PALETTE["sidebar"], highlightthickness=0, bd=0)
        nav_canvas.pack(fill="both", expand=True, pady=(18, 0))
        nav = ttk.Frame(nav_canvas, style="Sidebar.TFrame")
        nav_window = nav_canvas.create_window((0, 0), window=nav, anchor="nw")
        nav.bind(
            "<Configure>", lambda _event: nav_canvas.configure(scrollregion=nav_canvas.bbox("all"))
        )
        nav_canvas.bind(
            "<Configure>", lambda event: nav_canvas.itemconfigure(nav_window, width=event.width)
        )
        nav_scroller = WheelScroller(nav_canvas, nav)
        nav_canvas.bind("<Destroy>", lambda _event: nav_scroller.destroy(), add="+")
        for group_index, (group, keys) in enumerate(NAV_GROUPS):
            if group_index:
                ttk.Frame(nav, style="SidebarBorder.TFrame", height=1).pack(
                    fill="x", padx=24, pady=(18, 6)
                )
            ttk.Label(nav, text=group.upper(), style="GroupSidebar.TLabel").pack(
                fill="x", padx=(24, 0), pady=(8, 8)
            )
            for key in keys:
                if key == "users" and self.user["role"] != "admin":
                    continue
                entry = next(item for item in NAV_PAGES if item[0] == key)
                _key, label, icon, _page = entry
                button = Button(
                    nav,
                    text=f"{icon}   {label}",
                    style="Sidebar.TButton",
                    command=lambda bound=key: self.show_page(bound),
                )
                button.pack(fill="x", padx=16, pady=3)
                self.buttons[key] = button
        # A canvas hosting a window item still *requests* its ~1 px default,
        # not the content's height — and the content frame's requisition is
        # only computed at idle time. Settle it, then make the canvas ask for
        # exactly what the menu needs: pack allocates the canvas what the old
        # plain frame used to get, capping it at the cavity when space is
        # short (the overflow then scrolls, which is the point of the canvas).
        nav.update_idletasks()
        nav_canvas.configure(height=nav.winfo_reqheight())

        user_card = ttk.Frame(sidebar, style="SidebarBorder.TFrame", padding=1)
        user_card.pack(side="bottom", fill="x", padx=18, pady=18)
        inner = ttk.Frame(user_card, style="SidebarCard.TFrame", padding=(14, 14))
        inner.pack(fill="both", expand=True)
        identity = ttk.Frame(inner, style="SidebarCard.TFrame")
        identity.pack(fill="x")
        avatar_size = round(40 * ui_scale())
        avatar = tk.Canvas(
            identity,
            width=avatar_size,
            height=avatar_size,
            background=PALETTE["sidebar_hover"],
            highlightthickness=0,
            bd=0,
        )
        avatar.pack(side="left")
        initials = (
            "".join(part[0] for part in str(self.user["full_name"]).split()[:2]).upper() or "?"
        )
        avatar.create_oval(
            1, 1, avatar_size - 1, avatar_size - 1, fill=PALETTE["sidebar_active"], outline=""
        )
        avatar.create_text(
            avatar_size / 2,
            avatar_size / 2 + 1,
            text=initials,
            font=FONTS["micro_bold"],
            fill="#FFFFFF",
        )
        who = ttk.Frame(identity, style="SidebarCard.TFrame")
        who.pack(side="left", padx=(12, 0))
        ttk.Label(who, text=self.user["full_name"], style="TitleSidebarCard.TLabel").pack(
            anchor="w"
        )
        ttk.Label(who, text=self.user["role"].title(), style="MutedSidebarCard.TLabel").pack(
            anchor="w"
        )
        Button(inner, text="Sign out", style="SidebarGhost.TButton", command=self._sign_out).pack(
            fill="x", pady=(13, 0)
        )

        ttk.Frame(self, style="CardBorder.TFrame", width=1).pack(side="left", fill="y")

    def _build_status_bar(self) -> None:
        bar = ttk.Frame(self, style="Bar.TFrame", padding=(20, 12))
        bar.pack(side="bottom", fill="x")
        ttk.Frame(self, style="CardBorder.TFrame", height=1).pack(side="bottom", fill="x")
        dot_size = round(14 * ui_scale())
        self.dot = tk.Canvas(
            bar,
            width=dot_size,
            height=dot_size,
            background=PALETTE["surface"],
            highlightthickness=0,
            bd=0,
        )
        self.dot.pack(side="left", pady=0)
        self.status = ttk.Label(
            bar, text="Checking internet connection...", style="MutedBar.TLabel"
        )
        self.status.pack(side="left", padx=(10, 0))
        self._pack_separator(bar)
        self.organisation = tk.StringVar(value=self.store.settings().get("organisation", APP_NAME))
        ttk.Label(bar, textvariable=self.organisation, style="MutedBar.TLabel").pack(side="left")
        root_text = str(DATA_ROOT)
        if len(root_text) > 42:
            root_text = "..." + root_text[-41:]
        ttk.Label(bar, text=root_text, style="MicroBar.TLabel").pack(side="right", padx=(0, 22))
        self._pack_separator(bar, side="right")
        ttk.Label(
            bar,
            text=f"{self.user['full_name']}  ·  {self.user['role'].title()}",
            style="MutedBar.TLabel",
        ).pack(side="right")

    # -- pages ------------------------------------------------------------
    def show_page(self, key: str) -> None:
        if self.current and self.current in self.pages:
            self.pages[self.current].pack_forget()
        self.current = key
        page = self.pages.get(key)
        if page is None:
            try:
                page = PAGE_BY_KEY[key](self.container, self)
            except Exception:
                log.exception("Failed to build page %r", key)
                messagebox.showerror(
                    APP_NAME,
                    f"The {LABEL_BY_KEY.get(key, key)} page could not be opened.",
                    parent=self,
                )
                return
            self.pages[key] = page
        page.pack(fill="both", expand=True)
        _safe_call(page.refresh, what=f"refresh of {key} page")
        for button_key, button in self.buttons.items():
            button.configure(
                style="SidebarActive.TButton" if button_key == key else "Sidebar.TButton"
            )

    def refresh_all(self) -> None:
        self.feed.sync()  # every dialog that touches the fleet updates the GPS feed too
        page = self.pages.get(self.current)
        if page is not None:
            page.refresh()
        self.organisation.set(self.store.settings().get("organisation", APP_NAME))

    # -- connectivity -----------------------------------------------------
    def _start_connectivity_check(self) -> None:
        worker = threading.Thread(target=self._connectivity_worker, daemon=True)
        worker.start()
        _safe_after(self, 5000, self._poll_connectivity, what="connectivity poll")

    def _connectivity_worker(self) -> None:
        import urllib.error
        import urllib.request

        while not self._closing.is_set():
            try:
                with urllib.request.urlopen("https://www.openstreetmap.org", timeout=3) as response:
                    self._online = response.getcode() == 200
            except (urllib.error.URLError, OSError, ValueError):
                self._online = False
            self._closing.wait(30)

    def _poll_connectivity(self) -> None:
        if not self.winfo_exists():
            return
        if self._online is None:
            color, text = PALETTE["muted"], "Checking internet connection..."
        elif self._online:
            color, text = PALETTE["ok"], "Internet connection available"
        else:
            color, text = (
                PALETTE["warn"],
                "Offline — every feature still works, the map is drawn locally",
            )
        self.dot.delete("all")
        # outer ring + inner dot reads as a clean status indicator on white
        size = max(2, int(self.dot.cget("width")))
        inner = round(size * 0.29)
        self.dot.create_oval(
            1, 1, size - 2, size - 2, outline=_mix(color, "#FFFFFF", 0.45), width=1
        )
        self.dot.create_oval(
            inner, inner, size - 1 - inner, size - 1 - inner, fill=color, outline=""
        )
        self.status.configure(text=text)
        _safe_after(self, 10000, self._poll_connectivity, what="connectivity poll")

    # -- cross-page helpers -------------------------------------------------
    def open_new_booking(
        self, customer: dict[str, Any] | None = None, vehicle: dict[str, Any] | None = None
    ) -> None:
        safe_dialog(
            self,
            lambda: BookingDialog(
                self, self.store, customer=customer, vehicle=vehicle, actor=self.user["username"]
            ),
            on_success=self.refresh_all,
        )

    def open_payment_dialog(self, booking_id: int | None = None) -> None:
        safe_dialog(
            self,
            lambda: PaymentDialog(
                self, self.store, booking_id=booking_id, username=self.user["username"]
            ),
            on_success=self.refresh_all,
        )

    def focus_vehicle_on_map(self, vehicle_id: int) -> None:
        self.show_page("gps")
        page = self.pages.get("gps")
        if page is not None:
            page.focus_vehicle(vehicle_id)

    def focus_customer_on_map(self, customer_id: int) -> None:
        self.show_page("gps")
        page = self.pages.get("gps")
        if page is not None:
            page.focus_customer(customer_id)

    def focus_point_on_map(self, lat: float, lon: float, span: float = 5.0) -> None:
        self.show_page("gps")
        page = self.pages.get("gps")
        if page is not None:
            page.focus_point(lat, lon, span)

    def open_bookings_for(self, query: str) -> None:
        """Jump to the bookings page pre-filtered on a plate or customer name."""
        self.show_page("bookings")
        page = self.pages.get("bookings")
        if page is not None:
            page.focus_query(query)

    def open_customers_for(self, query: str) -> None:
        """Jump to the customer directory pre-filtered on a name."""
        self.show_page("customers")
        page = self.pages.get("customers")
        if page is not None:
            page.focus_query(query)

    # -- scheduled work ------------------------------------------------------
    def _start_auto_scan(self) -> None:
        if self.store.setting("auto_scan", "1") != "1":
            return
        _safe_after(self, 15 * 60 * 1000, self._auto_scan_tick, what="auto flood scan")

    def _auto_scan_tick(self) -> None:
        if not self.winfo_exists() or self._closing.is_set():
            return
        if self.store.setting("auto_scan", "1") != "1":
            return
        created: list[str] = []
        try:
            created = self.store.run_flood_scan(self.user["username"])
        except Exception:  # one bad scan must never kill the schedule
            log.exception("Automatic flood scan failed")
            created = []
        if created:
            if self.store.setting("sound_alerts", "1") == "1":
                try:
                    self.winfo_toplevel().bell()
                except tk.TclError:
                    pass
            self.refresh_all()
        _safe_after(self, 15 * 60 * 1000, self._auto_scan_tick, what="auto flood scan")

    def _backup(self) -> None:
        stamp = datetime.now().strftime("%Y%m%d_%H%M%S")
        destination = filedialog.asksaveasfilename(
            parent=self,
            title="Backup database",
            defaultextension=".db",
            initialfile=f"rental_system_backup_{stamp}.db",
            filetypes=[("SQLite database", "*.db"), ("All files", "*.*")],
        )
        if not destination:
            return
        try:
            saved = self.store.backup(destination)
        except (RuntimeError, sqlite3.Error) as error:
            messagebox.showerror(
                APP_NAME, f"The backup could not be created:\n{error}", parent=self
            )
            return
        messagebox.showinfo(APP_NAME, f"The database was backed up to:\n{saved}", parent=self)

    def _about(self) -> None:
        messagebox.showinfo(
            APP_NAME,
            f"{APP_NAME} v{APP_VERSION}\n\nRental operations with flood-risk monitoring"
            f" for Metro Manila.\n\nInterface: Tkinter · Storage: SQLite · Map data: OpenStreetMap\n"
            f"Data folder: {DATA_ROOT}\n\n"
            f"Default login: {DEFAULT_ADMIN_USERNAME} / {DEFAULT_ADMIN_PASSWORD}",
            parent=self,
        )

    def _sign_out(self) -> None:
        if not messagebox.askyesno(
            APP_NAME, "Sign out and return to the sign-in window?", parent=self
        ):
            return
        self.sign_out = True
        self.close()

    def close(self) -> None:
        if not self.sign_out and self.store.setting("confirm_exit", "1") == "1":
            if not messagebox.askyesno(APP_NAME, "Quit Flood Ready Vehicle System?", parent=self):
                return
        self._closing.set()
        self.feed.stop()
        self.winfo_toplevel().quit()


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Smoke test + launcher
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def run_smoke_test(log_path: str) -> int:
    """Build every page offscreen and exercise the services."""
    print(f"{APP_NAME} {APP_VERSION} — smoke test")
    print("-" * 56)
    failures: list[str] = []
    try:
        store = Store()
        print(" [ok] folders, database, schema and seed data")
    except Exception as error:
        print(f" [FAIL] database preparation: {type(error).__name__}: {error}")
        print(f"\nTechnical details were written to {log_path}")
        return 1

    def _status_guards() -> None:
        """Completed rentals and manual 'rented' flags must be rejected."""
        completed = store.row("SELECT id FROM bookings WHERE status = 'completed' LIMIT 1")
        if completed is not None:
            try:
                store.set_booking_status(int(completed["id"]), "confirmed")
            except ValueError:
                pass
            else:
                raise AssertionError("a completed booking accepted a status transition")
        free = store.row(
            "SELECT id FROM vehicles WHERE status = 'available' AND id NOT IN"
            " (SELECT vehicle_id FROM bookings"
            " WHERE status IN ('pending', 'confirmed', 'ongoing')) LIMIT 1"
        )
        if free is not None:
            try:
                store.set_vehicle_status(int(free["id"]), "rented")
            except ValueError:
                pass
            else:
                raise AssertionError("a unit was marked rented without an ongoing booking")

    def _booking_workflow() -> None:
        """Drive one scratch booking through the full rental lifecycle."""
        vehicle = store.row(
            "SELECT * FROM vehicles WHERE status = 'available' AND id NOT IN"
            " (SELECT vehicle_id FROM bookings"
            " WHERE status IN ('pending', 'confirmed', 'ongoing')) LIMIT 1"
        )
        customer = store.row("SELECT id FROM customers LIMIT 1")
        if vehicle is None or customer is None:
            return
        today = datetime.now().strftime(DATE_FORMAT)
        soon = (datetime.now() + timedelta(days=2)).strftime(DATE_FORMAT)
        later = (datetime.now() + timedelta(days=5)).strftime(DATE_FORMAT)
        booking_id = store.add_booking(
            {
                "vehicle_id": vehicle["id"],
                "customer_id": customer["id"],
                "start_date": today,
                "end_date": soon,
                "status": "pending",
            }
        )
        try:
            store.set_booking_status(booking_id, "confirmed")
            store.set_booking_status(booking_id, "ongoing")
            flag = store.value("SELECT status FROM vehicles WHERE id = ?", (vehicle["id"],))
            if flag != "rented":
                raise AssertionError("starting a rental did not mark the unit rented")
            days = store.extend_booking(booking_id, later)
            if days < 2:
                raise AssertionError("extend_booking returned an unexpected duration")
            store.set_booking_status(booking_id, "completed")
            flag = store.value("SELECT status FROM vehicles WHERE id = ?", (vehicle["id"],))
            if flag != "available":
                raise AssertionError("completing a rental did not free the unit")
        finally:
            store.execute("DELETE FROM transactions WHERE booking_id = ?", (booking_id,))
            store.execute("DELETE FROM bookings WHERE id = ?", (booking_id,))
            store.sync_vehicle_status(int(vehicle["id"]))

    def _overlap_guards() -> None:
        """Double-bookings must be rejected; back-to-back rentals must pass."""
        vehicle = store.row(
            "SELECT * FROM vehicles WHERE status = 'available' AND id NOT IN"
            " (SELECT vehicle_id FROM bookings"
            " WHERE status IN ('pending', 'confirmed', 'ongoing')) LIMIT 1"
        )
        customer = store.row("SELECT id FROM customers LIMIT 1")
        if vehicle is None or customer is None:
            return
        today = datetime.now().strftime(DATE_FORMAT)
        soon = (datetime.now() + timedelta(days=2)).strftime(DATE_FORMAT)
        after = (datetime.now() + timedelta(days=4)).strftime(DATE_FORMAT)
        booking_id = store.add_booking(
            {
                "vehicle_id": vehicle["id"],
                "customer_id": customer["id"],
                "start_date": today,
                "end_date": soon,
                "status": "pending",
            }
        )
        try:
            try:
                store.add_booking(
                    {
                        "vehicle_id": vehicle["id"],
                        "customer_id": customer["id"],
                        "start_date": today,
                        "end_date": soon,
                        "status": "pending",
                    }
                )
            except ValueError:
                pass
            else:
                raise AssertionError("an overlapping booking was accepted")
            back_to_back = store.add_booking(
                {
                    "vehicle_id": vehicle["id"],
                    "customer_id": customer["id"],
                    "start_date": soon,
                    "end_date": after,
                    "status": "pending",
                }
            )
            try:
                store.extend_booking(booking_id, after)
            except ValueError:
                pass
            else:
                raise AssertionError("an extension overlapping another booking was accepted")
            store.execute("DELETE FROM bookings WHERE id = ?", (back_to_back,))
        finally:
            store.execute("DELETE FROM transactions WHERE booking_id = ?", (booking_id,))
            store.execute("DELETE FROM bookings WHERE id = ?", (booking_id,))
            store.sync_vehicle_status(int(vehicle["id"]))

    for label, check in (
        ("authentication", lambda: store.login(DEFAULT_ADMIN_USERNAME, DEFAULT_ADMIN_PASSWORD)),
        ("dashboard statistics", store.dashboard),
        ("flood risk scan", lambda: store.run_flood_scan(DEFAULT_ADMIN_USERNAME)),
        ("exposure distances", store.exposure),
        ("report builders", lambda: [store.report(key) for key in store.REPORTS]),
        ("booking workflow", _booking_workflow),
        ("double-booking guards", _overlap_guards),
        ("status guards", _status_guards),
    ):
        try:
            check()
            print(f" [ok] {label}")
        except Exception as error:
            failures.append(f"{label}: {type(error).__name__}: {error}")
            print(f" [FAIL] {label}: {type(error).__name__}: {error}")

    root = tk.Tk()
    root.withdraw()
    build_fonts(root)
    apply_theme(root)
    user = store.login(DEFAULT_ADMIN_USERNAME, DEFAULT_ADMIN_PASSWORD)
    try:
        window = MainWindow(root, store, user)
        for key, label, _icon, _page in NAV_PAGES:
            if key == "users" and user["role"] != "admin":
                continue
            try:
                window.show_page(key)
                root.update_idletasks()
                print(f" [ok] {label.lower()} page")
            except Exception as error:
                failures.append(f"{label.lower()} page: {type(error).__name__}: {error}")
                print(f" [FAIL] {label.lower()} page: {type(error).__name__}: {error}")
        try:
            window.feed.sync()
            untracked = [
                vehicle["plate_number"]
                for vehicle in store.vehicles()
                if int(vehicle["id"]) not in window.feed.units
            ]
            if untracked:
                failures.append(
                    f"feed detection: {len(untracked)} unit(s) not tracked: {', '.join(untracked[:4])}"
                )
                print(f" [FAIL] feed detection ({len(untracked)} unit(s) untracked)")
            else:
                print(f" [ok] feed detection ({len(window.feed.units)} units tracked live)")
        except Exception as error:
            failures.append(f"feed detection: {type(error).__name__}: {error}")
            print(f" [FAIL] feed detection: {type(error).__name__}: {error}")
        try:
            fleet = store.vehicles()
            gps_page = window.pages.get("gps")
            if gps_page is not None and fleet:
                gps_page.select(int(fleet[0]["id"]))
                gps_page.map.focus(int(fleet[0]["id"]))
                root.update_idletasks()
                print(" [ok] fleet list selection and pin focus")
            else:
                print(" [skip] fleet list selection (no gps page or empty fleet)")
        except Exception as error:
            failures.append(f"fleet selection: {type(error).__name__}: {error}")
            print(f" [FAIL] fleet selection: {type(error).__name__}: {error}")
        window.destroy()
    except Exception as error:
        failures.append(f"main window: {type(error).__name__}: {error}")
        print(f" [FAIL] main window: {type(error).__name__}: {error}")
    finally:
        root.destroy()

    print("-" * 56)
    if failures:
        print(f"{len(failures)} problem(s) found:")
        for failure in failures:
            print(f"  - {failure}")
        print(f"\nTechnical details were written to {log_path}")
        return 1
    print("Everything works. Start the application with:\n  python main.py")
    return 0


def _fatal(message: str, log_path: str) -> None:
    log.critical(message)
    try:
        root = tk.Tk()
        root.withdraw()
        messagebox.showerror(
            APP_NAME, f"{message}\n\nData folder: {DATA_DIR}\nLog file: {log_path}"
        )
        root.destroy()
    except Exception:  # pragma: no cover - no display available
        print(message, file=sys.stderr)


def main() -> int:
    log_path = setup_logging()
    sys.excepthook = lambda kind, value, trace: log.exception(
        "Unhandled exception", exc_info=(kind, value, trace)
    )
    if sys.platform == "win32":  # crisp text on high-DPI Windows displays
        try:
            import ctypes

            ctypes.windll.shcore.SetProcessDpiAwareness(1)
        except Exception:  # pragma: no cover - older Windows releases
            pass
    if "--smoke-test" in sys.argv[1:]:
        return run_smoke_test(str(log_path))

    try:
        store = Store()
    except Exception as error:
        log.exception("Startup failed")
        _fatal(
            f"The application could not prepare its local database.\n\n{type(error).__name__}: {error}",
            str(log_path),
        )
        return 1

    try:
        root = tk.Tk()
    except Exception as error:  # pragma: no cover - python without tcl/tk
        _fatal(
            "Python's Tkinter module is not available on this computer.\n\n"
            "Reinstall Python 3.11 or newer and make sure the 'tcl/tk and IDLE' "
            "option is ticked in the installer.",
            str(log_path),
        )
        log.debug("tkinter import error: %s", error)
        return 1

    build_fonts(root)
    apply_theme(root)
    root.withdraw()

    try:
        while True:
            login = LoginWindow(root, store)
            root.wait_window(login)
            if login.user is None:
                root.destroy()
                return 0
            window = MainWindow(root, store, login.user)
            root.deiconify()
            root.mainloop()
            window.destroy()
            if not window.sign_out:
                root.destroy()
                return 0
            root.withdraw()
    except Exception as error:
        log.exception("Interface failure")
        _fatal(
            f"The application interface could not be started.\n\n{type(error).__name__}: {error}",
            str(log_path),
        )
        return 1
    finally:
        log.info("Application closed")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())
